Package manager knowledge base

The facts depcurfew relies on, each linked to the official documentation or changelog it was verified against.

Legend: ↗ source · ❓ uncertain (official sources are incomplete) · ⚠️ disputed (official sources disagree; every variant is shown) · 1.2.3+ means "from version 1.2.3".

12 tools · 697 facts · 102 uncertain · 14 disputed · verified 2026-10-28 to 2026-11-01.

  • python.bundledPip (✅ verified): {"line":"3.9","latest":"3.9.25","bundles":[["3.9.0","20.2.3"],["3.9.5","21.1.1"],["3.9.6","21.1.3"],["3.9.7","21.2.3"],["3.9.8","21.2.4"],["3.9.11","22.0.4"],["3.9.17","23.0.1"]]}, {"line":"3.10","latest":"3.10.22","bundles":[["3.10.0","21.2.3"],["3.10.1","21.2.4"],["3.10.3","22.0.4"],["3.10.6","22.2.1"],["3.10.7","22.2.2"],["3.10.9","22.3.1"],["3.10.11","23.0.1"]]}, {"line":"3.11","latest":"3.11.18","bundles":[["3.11.0","22.3.0"],["3.11.1","22.3.1"],["3.11.4","23.1.2"],["3.11.5","23.2.1"],["3.11.8","24.0.0"]]}, {"line":"3.12","latest":"3.12.16","bundles":[["3.12.0","23.2.1"],["3.12.2","24.0.0"],["3.12.5","24.2.0"],["3.12.8","24.3.1"],["3.12.10","25.0.1"]]}, {"line":"3.13","latest":"3.13.18","bundles":[["3.13.0","24.2.0"],["3.13.1","24.3.1"],["3.13.3","25.0.1"],["3.13.4","25.1.1"],["3.13.6","25.2.0"],["3.13.10","25.3.0"],["3.13.13","26.0.1"],["3.13.14","26.1.2"],["3.13.15","26.2.1"],["3.13.17","26.2.2"]]}, {"line":"3.14","latest":"3.14.10","bundles":[["3.14.0","25.2.0"],["3.14.1","25.3.0"],["3.14.4","26.0.1"],["3.14.5","26.1.1"],["3.14.6","26.1.2"],["3.14.7","26.2.1"],["3.14.9","26.2.2"]]}, {"line":"3.15","latest":"3.15.2","bundles":[["3.15.0","26.2.1"],["3.15.1","26.2.2"]]} ↗

  • python.interpreter (✅ verified): {"executables":["python","python3","py"],"moduleOption":"-m","commandOption":"-c","valueOptions":["-W","-X","--check-hash-based-pycs"],"groupedShortOptions":true} ↗

Notes

Context recorded with verified facts.

npm

  • cooldown.configFiles: Any npm config layer works: project .npmrc (read only at the project root and not for global installs), ~/.npmrc, the global npmrc, npm_config_min_release_age or the CLI. package.json cannot set it. ↗
  • cooldown.unit: Type is null or Number; 0 turns the delay off. Fractional days are not documented. ↗
  • cooldown.default: Unchanged in npm 12. ↗
  • cooldown.securityUpdates: npm audit fix keeps the vulnerable version, warns that the fix was blocked (since 11.18.0) and exits non-zero. Add the package to min-release-age-exclude or relax the delay. ↗
  • installScripts.defaultPolicy: strict-allow-scripts (default false) turns a dependency script that allowScripts does not cover "from a warning into a hard error ... instead of running with a notice", so from 11.16.0 such scripts still run. npm 11.21.1 ran esbuild's postinstall on 2026-10-29 ("has install scripts not yet covered by allowScripts"); npm 12.2.1 blocked it ("had install scripts blocked"). The v11 install-scripts command page says scripts are blocked by default, which the config definition, the 11.16.0 pull request, the GitHub changelog and the test contradict. npm 12 also blocks implicit node-gyp builds and prepare scripts of git, file and link dependencies. ↗
  • installScripts.allowList.file: Only the root package.json counts; allowScripts in dependencies is ignored. npx, npm exec and global installs use the allow-scripts config instead. ↗
  • installScripts.disableAll: Default false. Overrides allowScripts and dangerously-allow-all-scripts. npm run/test/start still run the named script, but not its pre/post scripts. ↗
  • sources.git.values: root allows only git dependencies listed in the project package.json. ↗
  • sources.remoteTarball.values: Tarballs on the configured registry hostname are always allowed. The 2026-05-22 GitHub changelog mentions only all and none; the config reference also lists root. ↗
  • sources.file.values: The 2026-05-22 GitHub changelog mentions only all and none; the config reference also lists root. ↗
  • sources.file.default: npm 12 did not change the default. ↗
  • sources.directory.values: The 2026-05-22 GitHub changelog mentions only all and none; the config reference also lists root. ↗
  • sources.directory.default: npm 12 did not change the default. ↗
  • project.lockfiles: npm 11 and earlier prefer npm-shrinkwrap.json over package-lock.json when both exist; npm 12 no longer reads or writes npm-shrinkwrap.json (same format, rename it). ↗
  • project.workspaces: An array of folder paths or globs. ↗
  • user.files: userconfig defaults to ~/.npmrc ("The location of user-level configuration settings. This may be overridden by the npm_config_userconfig environment variable or the --userconfig command line option"); ~ is resolved against this.env.HOME || homedir(). Same in 11.21.1. The project .npmrc is loaded first and may set userconfig= itself. Only the keys above the first [section] header are settings (extra.npmrcFormat). ↗
  • user.settings: Every config definition is read from any config file; only prefix is refused in a project .npmrc. allowScripts in package.json is project-only: at user level the allow-list is the allow-scripts key (allow only, every name becomes true; resolve-allow-scripts.js), used where the root package.json has no allowScripts and for npx, npm exec and npm install -g. The source keys use the later of the disputed first versions. ↗
  • user.unknownKeys: Unknown .npmrc (file) configs warn by default, matching pre-npm-12 behavior, but error when strict-npmrc is set. ↗
  • user.invalidValueFails: A value of the wrong type is reported as invalid config and the default is used. ↗
  • user.weakening: A key in the project .npmrc replaces the user value, lists included (prototype chain, no merging); the CLI and npm_config_* variables beat both. dangerously-allow-all-scripts is on for every value but false, null and undefined: npm reads .npmrc values and npm_config_* variables with parseField (https://github.com/npm/cli/blob/v11.21.1/workspaces/config/lib/parse-field.js), which trims them, turns an empty value of a Boolean setting into true and only "true", "false", "null" and "undefined" into their values, and leaves 0, 1, yes or no as strings; load() does not run the nopt type check (validate() runs only in npm config), and arborist tests the flat option for truth (!this.options.dangerouslyAllowAllScripts in workspaces/arborist/lib/arborist/rebuild.js at 11.21.1). ↗
  • user.variables: Environment variables starting with npm_config_ are interpreted as configuration parameters; config values are case-insensitive, so NPM_CONFIG_FOO=bar works the same. loadEnv in workspaces/config/lib/index.js (11.21.1 and 12.2.1) skips variables whose value is empty and reads them in the order of the environment, so of two spellings the later one wins. userconfig defaults to ~/.npmrc (definitions.js), and npm exports the resolved path as npm_config_userconfig to every command it runs (see extra.childEnvironment), so that value names the default file. ↗
  • extra.localPrefix: loadLocalPrefix: --prefix on the command line is the local prefix. Otherwise npm walks up from the current directory (walk-up-path yields the directory and each parent up to the root, with no other stop; "for (const p of walkUp(this.cwd))") and takes the first directory with a package.json file or a node_modules directory ("hasPackageJson || await dirExists(p, 'node_modules')"). Unless workspaces are set to false on the command line ("this.#get('workspaces', 'cli')", so --no-workspaces or --workspaces=false; .npmrc and npm_config_workspaces do not count) or npm runs in global mode, it walks on: each higher directory with a package.json is read with @npmcli/package-json normalize, and one that cannot be read or normalized (".catch(() => ({ content: {} }))") or has no workspaces is passed over; the first whose mapped workspaces include that directory ("w === this.localPrefix", the same path) becomes the prefix, and a .npmrc of the workspace is skipped ("ignoring workspace config at ${this.localPrefix}/.npmrc"). Without a match the prefix is the current directory ("this.localPrefix = this.cwd"). loadProjectConfig reads "resolve(this.localPrefix, '.npmrc')" as the project configuration, except in global mode and when that path is the userconfig file. Workspaces (@npmcli/map-workspaces 6.0.0, the version npm 12.2.1 locks, with glob 13.0.6 and minimatch 10.2.5, https://github.com/npm/map-workspaces/blob/v6.0.0/lib/index.js): an array of patterns or an object with a packages array, else EWORKSPACESCONFIG, and an entry that is not text throws too; the leading ! are removed and an odd number of them negates, then a leading ./ or leading slashes ("pattern.replace(/^.?/+/, '')"); a pattern drops the earlier negated patterns it matches as text, and each negated pattern drops the patterns it matches as text and is given to glob as ignore, with **/node_modules/**; glob gets every pattern with a trailing / (directories only) and its defaults: * and ** do not match a name that starts with a dot, ** does not follow symlinked directories, and matching ignores case on macOS and Windows; a match without package.json (ENOENT, ENOTDIR) is no workspace, another error and two workspaces of one name throw, and npm stops. normalize (https://github.com/npm/package-json/blob/v8.0.0/lib/normalize.js, steps bin and binDir) throws for a bin list with an entry that is not text and for directories.bin that is not text when bin is not set. No .git or home directory stops the walk. ↗
  • extra.childEnvironment: setEnvs gives the scripts and commands npm runs (npm run, npx, npm exec) every setting that is not its default as npm_config_<key> in lower case with - as _, whichever file or flag set it, unless the definition has envExport: false (allow-scripts, min-release-age and min-release-age-exclude in 11.21.1 and 12.2.1); keys npm does not know, such as pnpm keys in .npmrc, are exported too. userconfig is always exported, because its default is the literal ~/.npmrc and the value the resolved path. It also sets npm_execpath to the npm it runs. The same in 10.9.4 and 12.2.1 (12.x and 11.x also export npm_config_node_gyp and npm_config_npm_version). ↗
  • extra.denyList: A false entry in the root package.json allowScripts is an explicit denial: deny wins over allow, and denied packages are skipped silently, also under strict-allow-scripts ("const verdict = isScriptAllowed(node, policy); if (verdict === true || verdict === false) { continue }", the same in 11.16.0 check-allow-scripts.js). npm install-scripts deny writes a name-only false entry. ↗
  • extra.trustedPublishing: Publishing from GitHub-hosted runners with OIDC instead of a token. Provenance is generated automatically, only for a public package from a public repository. A new trusted publisher configuration expires unless its first publish succeeds within 2 days. It can be limited to npm stage publish (the default for configurations created after 2026-09-03). ↗
  • extra.stagedPublishing: Staging needs no 2FA, so CI can do it; approving or rejecting needs 2FA (CLI or npmjs.com). Staging a package that does not exist yet creates it with the public placeholder version 0.0.0-stage. Pre-release versions need an explicit --tag. --provenance also applies to npm stage publish. Rejecting the only staged version of such a new package also unpublished the placeholder (observed 2026-10-29 with npm 11.21.1), and after a whole package is unpublished its name is blocked for 24 hours (https://github.com/npm/documentation/blob/main/content/policies/unpublish.mdx). ↗
  • extra.trustCommand: Configures a trusted publisher from the CLI; needs 2FA on the account and write access, and the package must already exist. At least one permission flag is required. ↗
  • extra.lockfileFormat: Entries under packages: resolved is the registry tarball URL, the git URL with its commit (git+ssh://… or git+https://…#sha), file:<path> for local tarballs and directories, or the link target when link is true. hasInstallScript marks a preinstall, install or postinstall script (prepare scripts of non-registry sources are not flagged). npm tells registry tarballs from other URLs with the registryTarball pattern (workspaces/arborist/lib/install-scripts.js), so a tarball URL on a registry host looks like a registry package. An entry without resolved is installed as <name>@<version> (workspaces/arborist/lib/arborist/reify.js, #extractOrLink, in 11.19.1 and 12.2.1), so its version field is the source then. Entries keep the dependency fields of their package.json: dependencies, optionalDependencies and peerDependencies for every package, devDependencies for the root (workspaces/arborist/lib/shrinkwrap.js, pkgMetaKeys and the isTop branch, in 11.21.1 and 12.2.1); a dependency declared there as a URL comes from that URL even when it is a registry tarball. ↗
  • extra.overrides: Only the root package.json counts. Keys are package names, optionally with a version or range (name@range), and nest to any depth to scope an override under a parent; "." inside a nested object overrides the parent package itself; values take any dependency specifier, including tarball URLs, and $name refers to the spec of a direct dependency. Overrides are not written to package-lock.json (checked with npm 11.21.1), so a URL override of a registry tarball looks like a registry package there. With replace-registry-host at its default npmjs, npm writes the configured registry in place of registry.npmjs.org in resolved URLs (workspaces/config/lib/definitions/definitions.js). ↗
  • extra.npmrcScope: Only the .npmrc at the root of the project npm runs in is read. Inside a workspace package npm moves the prefix to the workspace root (using-npm/workspaces.md) and warns "ignoring workspace config" for the package .npmrc (workspaces/config/lib/index.js). The project .npmrc "is not read in global mode, such as when running npm install -g" (same page, v11.21.1 and v12.2.1). ↗
  • extra.npmrcFormat: npm reads .npmrc with the ini package: 7.0.0 in npm 12.2.1, and 6.0.0, whose lib/ini.js is the same, in npm 11.10.0 to 11.21.1 (package-lock.json of npm/cli). A line whose first non-blank character is # or ; is a comment. An unquoted key or value ends at the first # or ; that is not escaped with a backslash, so ignore-scripts=true # note sets true; a value in double quotes is parsed as a JSON string and one in single quotes loses its quotes, both keeping # and ;. A line without = sets the key to true. A [name] header at the start of a line, with nothing but spaces after it, starts a section: the keys below it belong to that section until the next header. npm applies only the top-level keys as settings (#loadObject in workspaces/config/lib/index.js at v12.2.1), so a key below a header has no effect. ↗
  • extra.npmrcListSyntax: Array values are written by adding [] after the key, one line per value, e.g. min-release-age-exclude[]=@myorg/*. ↗
  • extra.allowAllScripts: Boolean, default false: runs every dependency install script whether or not allowScripts approves it; --ignore-scripts still wins. First present in the 11.16.0 definitions. ↗
  • extra.excludeWildcards: min-release-age-exclude takes package names or minimatch globs from its first version. ↗
  • extra.latestVersion: Marked as the latest GitHub release on 2026-10-28; the 11.x line is still maintained (11.21.1). ↗
  • extra.beforeInteraction: before is a date: npm installs only versions available on or before it. In 11.10.0 to 11.14.x the definitions of min-release-age and before name each other as exclusive, and npm stops with "--min-release-age cannot be provided when using --before" (or the reverse) once both are set in its config sources (#loadObject in workspaces/config/lib/index.js and definitions.js at v11.14.1). Since 11.15.0 both may be set: "If before and min-release-age are both set in the same source, before wins"; across sources the usual precedence applies (cli > env > project > user > global), so a before in the project .npmrc replaces its own min-release-age (definitions.js at v11.15.0 and v12.2.1; release notes of 11.15.0). ↗
  • extra.strictAllowScripts: strict-allow-scripts=true makes the install fail on a dependency with install scripts that allowScripts does not cover, instead of running them with a notice (default false). Entries denied with false are skipped silently; --ignore-scripts and --dangerously-allow-all-scripts override it. ↗
  • extra.strictAllowScriptsEffect: npm 12.2.1 still defines strict-allow-scripts (default false): "If true, turn the install-script policy from a warning into a hard error: any dependency with install scripts that is not covered by allowScripts will fail the install instead of being blocked with a warning." Dependencies denied with false are still skipped silently; --ignore-scripts and --dangerously-allow-all-scripts override it. So the same .npmrc line keeps npm 11.16 to 11.x from running such scripts and makes npm 12 stop the install at them. The 11.16.0 effect is the one in strictAllowScripts (definitions.js at v11.16.0). ↗
  • extra.unknownNpmrcKeys: Matters for generated .npmrc files: keys npm does not know (e.g. pnpm-only keys) print a warning on every npm command. The 12.0.0 entry: unknown CLI flags "now throw instead of warning. (Unknown .npmrc configs still warn by default; opt into erroring with the new strict-npmrc config.)"; lib/base-cmd.js at 12.2.1 does the same. ↗
  • extra.commandLine: Aliases of install (add, i and the typo aliases in, ins, inst, insta, instal, isnt, isnta, isntal, isntall), install-test (it), exec (x) and init (create, innit), the same in 11.19.1. install-test takes the same arguments as install (docs/lib/content/commands/npm-install-test.md). npm exec and npx take the packages to download from --package; -p means --package only in npx (in npm it is --parseable); without --package the first positional argument names the package, and -c/--call takes a command string that runs with the packages, shell operators included (npm-exec.md). npm init <initializer> runs npm exec create-<initializer>: foo → create-foo, @usr → @usr/create, @usr/foo@2.0.0 → @usr/create-foo@2.0.0 (npm-init.md). ↗
  • extra.valueOptions: npm options that always take a value: every definition whose type does not include Boolean, as --name, and the short forms -C (prefix), -L (location), -c (call), -m (message) and -w (workspace). Options whose type includes Boolean (such as color and browser) may stand alone, so they are taken for flags. ↗
  • extra.flagOptions: npm options whose type includes Boolean, which may stand alone: the definitions of 12.2.1 and of 11.21.1 (where shrinkwrap is one too; 12.2.1 adds ignore-existing, ignore-extension, ignore-patch-failures, allow-unused-patches, keep-edit-dir and strict-npmrc). With valueOptions they are every npm option, so an abbreviated option can be resolved as npm resolves it. ↗
  • extra.parsing: The shorthands npm passes to nopt: the list in definitions/index.js and the short names of the definitions (the same in 11.21.1). nopt (node_modules/nopt/lib/nopt-lib.js, versions 8.1.0 in npm 10.9.4, 9.0.0 in 11.21.1 and 10.0.1 in 12.2.1, same parse and resolveShort) strips every leading dash, so -reg, --reg and -registry work; takes an exact option first, then an exact shorthand, then a word of one-letter shorthands (-gD), then an unambiguous prefix of an option name (abbrev), then a prefix of a shorthand; and reads --no-<name> as a negated flag that takes no value. After the shorthands (resolveShort) nopt strips every leading no- in any case, each one inverting the flag, and then takes <name> as an exact option or an unambiguous prefix (abbrevs), so --no-ign is --no-ignore-scripts where no other option starts with ign (npm 11; npm 12 has ignore-existing too) and --no-no-ignore-scripts sets ignore-scripts to true; a negated flag takes a following true or false, inverted (definitions/index.js and definitions.js at v12.2.1 and lib/nopt-lib.js of nopt 10.0.1, read again 2026-11-01). npm 10 expands abbreviations silently, npm 11 warns "Expanding --regis to --registry. This will stop working in the next major version of npm." and npm 12 refuses them with EUNKNOWNCONFIG (abbrevHandler in workspaces/config/lib/index.js of 11.21.1 and 12.2.1). Shorthands are expanded only on the command line, not in .npmrc or npm_config_ variables. npx turns -p into --package and expands exact shorthands itself before npm exec reads the rest (bin/npx-cli.js). ↗
  • extra.versionSelection: tag (default latest): "If you ask npm to install a package and don't tell it a specific version, then it will install the specified tag"; like any setting it comes from --tag, npm_config_tag or .npmrc. A name without a version is name@* (npm-package-arg 14.0.0, lib/npa.js). npm-pick-manifest 12.0.0 (node_modules/npm-pick-manifest/lib/index.js) picks the version of that dist-tag when the range is * or the version satisfies the range, unless it is deprecated; otherwise the highest satisfying version, preferring versions that are not deprecated; prereleases match a range only when it names one, so name@* with latest on a prerelease installs that prerelease. npm exec and npm init pick with the same settings. ↗
  • extra.packageSpecs: Forms of npm install <spec>: a folder; a tarball file, whose name must end in .tar, .tar.gz or .tgz; a tarball URL, which must start with http:// or https://; a git remote URL with the protocol git, git+ssh, git+http, git+https or git+file; <githubname>/<githubrepo> or github:<githubname>/<githubrepo>; gist:, bitbucket: and gitlab: shorthands; <alias>@npm:<name>. The file: prefix for local paths is documented in docs/lib/content/configuring-npm/package-json.md (Local Paths). ↗
  • extra.registryConfig: userconfig, the location of user-level settings, defaults to ~/.npmrc; registry is the base URL of the npm registry (default https://registry.npmjs.org/); a per-project .npmrc can set it like any config key (docs/lib/content/using-npm/config.md). npm config set @myco:registry=http://reg.example.com associates a scope with a registry, and any npm install of a package with that scope then requests it from there (https://docs.npmjs.com/cli/v12/using-npm/scope#associating-a-scope-with-a-registry). ↗
  • extra.registryOverrides: registry is a URL; userconfig and globalconfig are the user and global configuration files. Putting --foo bar on the command line sets the foo configuration parameter, and environment variables that start with npm_config_ are configuration parameters too, without regard to case (docs/lib/content/using-npm/config.md); @scope:registry is the key of a scope's registry (https://docs.npmjs.com/cli/v12/using-npm/scope). ↗
  • extra.configCommands: npm config set sets keys (in the user configuration unless location is given), delete (also rm and del) removes keys from every configuration file, edit opens a configuration file in an editor and fix repairs invalid items; get and list (ls) only read (the switch in exec, and docs/lib/content/commands/npm-config.md). npm set <key>=<value> runs npm config set with its arguments and fails without any (lib/commands/set.js); npm get only reads (lib/commands/get.js). npm pkg set, delete and fix write package.json back, get only reads (lib/commands/pkg.js). Other spellings of these commands come from commandNames. ↗
  • extra.commandNames: deref resolves a command name: camelCase to kebab-case (installTest is install-test), an exact command, a direct alias, else a unique abbreviation of the commands and alias names (abbrev), whose aliases are followed to the command (npm install-cl is install-clean, then ci). So npm c, npm con, npm conf and npm confi are npm config, and npm pk is npm pkg. ↗
  • extra.installOverrides: Settings that a single npm command can change on its command line or in its environment: "Putting --foo bar on the command line sets the foo configuration parameter", "--flag without specifying any value will set the value to true", "Any environment variables that start with npm_config_ will be interpreted as a configuration parameter", case-insensitive, and an environment configuration without a value is true (docs/lib/content/using-npm/config.md at v12.2.1). Types in definitions.js at v12.2.1: ignore-scripts Boolean, default false ("If true, npm does not run scripts specified in package.json files"); min-release-age null or Number of days; before null or Date; min-release-age-exclude a list exempt from min-release-age and before; dangerously-allow-all-scripts Boolean, which bypasses the allowScripts policy; allow-scripts a list of packages whose install scripts may run. A value other than true for ignore-scripts, any true value of dangerously-allow-all-scripts (the false values as in user.variables), any allow-scripts, before or min-release-age-exclude, and a min-release-age shorter than the policy weaken what depcurfew apply wrote. ↗
  • extra.directoryOptions: prefix (short C, so -C dir is --prefix dir): "The location to install global items. If set on the command line, then it forces non-global commands to run in the specified folder." loadLocalPrefix in workspaces/config/lib/index.js (v12.2.1) reads it only from the command line ("this.#get('prefix', 'cli')"), takes it as the local prefix as given and does not walk up, so npm_config_prefix and .npmrc do not move a command; the project .npmrc is the one in that folder. ↗
  • extra.booleanWords: nopt, which parses the npm command line, also supports "--bool true or --bool false": a Boolean option followed by the word true or false takes it as its value and skips it, and a --no- prefix inverts the value (parse in lib/nopt-lib.js at v10.0.1, used by npm 12.2.1, and the same code at v9.0.0, used by npm 11.21.1). ↗
  • extra.installCommands: Commands that install packages into the project and their aliases: install (add, i, typo aliases, install-test and it), ci (clean-install, ic, install-clean, isntall-clean) with install-ci-test (cit, clean-install-test, sit), and update (up, upgrade, udpate; u only from npm 12, so left out). The same in v10.9.9 (lib/utils/cmd-list.js). npm also accepts unique abbreviations of command names, which are not listed. ↗
  • extra.globalOption: global (short g) "Operates in global mode, so that packages are installed into the prefix folder instead of the current working directory" and sets location to global; npm install -g npm@<version> replaces the npm that runs (docs/lib/content/commands/npm-install.md: "In global mode (ie, with -g or --global appended to the command)"). ↗
  • extra.unknownConfigKeys: npm 10 loads every key of an .npmrc without checking whether it knows it (#loadObject only checks deprecated and exclusive keys), so a setting it does not have yet, such as min-release-age, is ignored without any message. checkUnknown first appears in v11.2.0 (workspaces/config/lib/index.js), which warns: Unknown project config "<key>". This will stop working in the next major version of npm. The 11.2.0 changelog does not name the change; the version comes from reading the code at the tags. npm 12 collects unknown keys to fail the command (v12.2.1), with the exact behavior not read. ↗
  • extra.cleanInstall: npm ci (the clean commands of installCommands): "The project must have an existing package-lock.json"; "If dependencies in the package lock do not match those in package.json, npm ci will exit with an error, instead of updating the package lock"; "It will never write to package.json or package-lock.json: installs are essentially frozen." lib/commands/ci.js at v12.2.1 loads the lockfile (loadVirtual), builds the ideal tree, throws "npm ci can only install packages when your package.json and package-lock.json are in sync" when validateLockfile finds a difference, and reifies with packageLock: true and save: false. So npm ci installs exactly the versions package-lock.json records, and a release-age delay cannot change which versions it installs; the settings of its params (ignore-scripts, allow-scripts, strict-allow-scripts, allow-git, allow-remote, allow-file, allow-directory) still apply. ↗
  • extra.scriptsOff: ignore-scripts (Boolean, default false; definitions.js at v12.2.1: "If true, npm does not run scripts specified in package.json files") is one of the params of npm ci and npm install (lib/commands/ci.js). On the command line --ignore-scripts sets it; "Any environment variables that start with npm_config_ will be interpreted as a configuration parameter", with underscores for dashes, and "Config values are case-insensitive, so NPM_CONFIG_FOO=bar will work the same". depcurfew counts only the value true (and the flag without a value) as off: parseField turns only "true" into true, and loadEnv skips empty values (user.variables). ↗
  • extra.nodeEngines: engines.node of npm/cli package.json, the same at the tags v11.0.0, v11.10.0, v11.16.0, v11.19.1 and v11.21.1 (^20.17.0 || >=22.9.0) and at v12.0.0 and v12.2.1 (^22.22.2 || ^24.15.0 || >=26.0.0, https://github.com/npm/cli/blob/v12.2.1/package.json). On a Node.js outside the range npm only warns that it does not support it: lib/cli/entry.js at v11.19.1 runs log.warn('cli', validateEngines.unsupportedMessage) when !satisfies(node, engines), for a Node.js "still unsupported and untested and might not work reliably", and lib/cli/validate-engines.js prints "ERROR: npm ... is known not to run on Node.js ..." when it fails with a SyntaxError. So npm install -g npm@11 on Node.js 18 installs an npm that is not meant to run there. ↗

pnpm

  • cooldown.configFiles: pnpm-workspace.yaml works in every version that has the setting. The global config.yaml exists only from 11.0.0 ("the new global config.yaml"); pnpm 10 reads user-level settings from ~/.npmrc and <configDir>/rc instead (see user.files). pnpm 10 also reads minimum-release-age from .npmrc; pnpm 11+ reads only auth and registry settings from .npmrc and ignores the package.json "pnpm" field. ↗
  • cooldown.unit: Integer number of minutes, e.g. 1440 = 1 day; no duration strings. ↗
  • cooldown.default: The built-in 1440 is non-strict: pnpm adds immature versions to minimumReleaseAgeExclude instead of failing (see strictMode). ↗
  • cooldown.securityUpdates: No setting bypasses the delay. Since 11.0.0, pnpm audit --fix adds the minimum patched version of each advisory to minimumReleaseAgeExclude (since 11.22.0 only while it is still inside the window). ↗
  • installScripts.defaultPolicy: Unreviewed builds only warn in pnpm 10; strictDepBuilds is true by default since 11.0.0, so they fail the install. Prepare scripts of git-hosted dependencies are blocked since 10.26.0. ↗
  • installScripts.allowList.setting: Replaced onlyBuiltDependencies, which was removed in 11.0.0 (see legacyAllowList). Since 12.11.0, permissions.<package>.build takes precedence over allowBuilds (extra.buildPermissions). ↗
  • installScripts.disableAll: Default false; --ignore-scripts on the CLI. Also skips the project's own scripts. ↗
  • sources.git.setting: Blocks git repositories and tarball URLs for transitive dependencies only; no setting blocks them as direct dependencies. ↗
  • sources.remoteTarball.setting: Blocks git repositories and tarball URLs for transitive dependencies only; no setting blocks them as direct dependencies. ↗
  • project.workspaces: Globs with ! exclusions. Without packages only the root is in the workspace; pnpm still reads settings from the file in single-package projects. pnpm ignores workspaces in package.json. ↗
  • user.files: getConfigDir: $XDG_CONFIG_HOME/pnpm when it is set, else ~/Library/Preferences/pnpm on macOS, %LOCALAPPDATA%/pnpm/config on Windows (~/.config/pnpm without LOCALAPPDATA) and ~/.config/pnpm elsewhere; the same in 10.34.9 (config/config/src/dirs.ts) and 12.17.0 (pnpm/crates/config-dir/src/lib.rs). pnpm 10 reads <configDir>/rc, INI with kebab-case keys (config/config/src/index.ts at 10.34.9; first version not tracked), and also ~/.npmrc for every setting, which beats rc: loadNpmConf reads the command line, npm_config_* variables, the project and workspace .npmrc, ~/.npmrc (userconfig, path.resolve(os.homedir(), ".npmrc") in https://github.com/pnpm/npm-conf/blob/v3.0.3/lib/defaults.js) and the global npmrc, and only then is rc added as "pnpm-global", below all of them (index.ts at 10.34.9). From 11.0.0 the settings file is <configDir>/config.yaml with camelCase keys (11.0.0 changelog: "the new global config.yaml"), and ~/.npmrc and <configDir>/auth.ini carry only auth, registry and network settings. The 11.x and 12.x docs page cli/config.md still names a global rc file for registry and auth settings, which that code no longer reads. ↗
  • user.settings: pnpm 10 reads the kebab-case keys from rc as from any .npmrc (config/config/src/types.ts: only-built-dependencies at 10.0.0, strict-dep-builds at 10.3.0, minimum-release-age and minimum-release-age-exclude at 10.16.0, block-exotic-subdeps at 10.26.0); since 10.13.0 build settings from these files apply only to projects whose package.json "pnpm" field and pnpm-workspace.yaml set none of dangerouslyAllowAllBuilds, onlyBuiltDependencies(File), neverBuiltDependencies or allowBuilds, and always to global installs. pnpm 11+ applies a config.yaml key only when isConfigFileKey accepts it and it is camelCase: the minimumReleaseAge keys and blockExoticSubdeps since 11.0.0, strictDepBuilds since 11.0.7 (in excludedPnpmKeys in 11.0.0-11.0.6). allowBuilds is project-only from 11.0.0 and dropped with a warning, so pnpm 11+ has no user-level allow-list. pnpm 12 silently drops blockExoticSubdeps from config.yaml although its key gate accepts it (clear_workspace_resolution_fields in pnpm/crates/config/src/workspace_yaml/workspace_scope.rs, 12.0.0 to 12.17.0). pnpm self-update takes the minimumReleaseAge settings only from config.yaml, the environment and the command line, never from the project. ↗
  • user.weakening: A key in pnpm-workspace.yaml replaces the user value, lists included (applyWorkspaceSetting: pnpmConfig[key] = value); PNPM_CONFIG_* variables and the command line beat both. In pnpm 10 the same keys in ~/.npmrc beat rc, and since 10.13.0 build settings from either file reach only projects without build settings of their own, and global installs (config/config/src/index.ts at 10.34.9; before 10.13.0 dangerously-allow-all-builds also joined a project's onlyBuiltDependencies). In config.yaml, dangerouslyAllowAllBuilds also overrides a project's allowBuilds: false in pnpm 11 and 12.0.0-12.11.x (see extra.allowAllBuilds). pnpm 10 has no default delay and its strict-dep-builds and block-exotic-subdeps default to false, so 0 or false there weakens nothing; trustLockfile is a global key since it appeared in 11.3.0. The switch that allows every build is on for any value pnpm does not read as off, because pnpm tests it for truth (see extra.allowAllBuilds): in config.yaml only false, 0, null and an empty string are off, so "false", yes and "true" (strings for js-yaml) are on; in rc and ~/.npmrc pnpm 10 parses values with parseField of @pnpm/npm-conf (lib/util.js at https://github.com/pnpm/npm-conf/blob/v3.0.2/lib/util.js, the same in 3.0.3, which pnpm 10.28.0 and 10.34.9 use), which turns only "false", "null" and "undefined" into falsy values, an empty value of a Boolean setting into true, and leaves yes, 1 and 0 as strings, which are true. ↗
  • user.variables: pnpm 11 reads pnpm_config_<key> in lower case or PNPM_CONFIG_<KEY> in upper case, not mixed case (getEnvKeySuffix), over pnpm-workspace.yaml and under the command line; pnpm 12 reads the same two forms (pnpm/crates/config/src/env_overlay.rs at 12.17.0). pnpm 11+ ignores npm_config_* except npm_config_userconfig, which then moves only the auth .npmrc. The 11.0.0 release notes (https://github.com/pnpm/pnpm/releases/tag/v11.0.0): "pnpm no longer reads npm_config_* environment variables", "Added support for loading environment variables whose names start with pnpm_config_ into config"; pnpm 10.34.9 reads pnpm_config_ only for verify-deps-before-run (config/config/src/index.ts). pnpm 10 reads npm_config_* in any case, skipping empty values (addEnv in lib/conf.js of @pnpm/npm-conf 3.0.3), under pnpm-workspace.yaml (npmrcPrecedence), and parses them with the same parseField as .npmrc, so npm_config_dangerously_allow_all_builds is off only for false, null and undefined (1 and yes stay true strings); its npm_config_userconfig defaults to ~/.npmrc (lib/defaults.js). pnpm 11 parses a Boolean pnpm_config_ value with parseBoolean of env.ts, which takes only true as true (false gives false, any other text no value). XDG_CONFIG_HOME and LOCALAPPDATA move the config directory (user.files). ↗
  • extra.denyList: allowBuilds: { pkg: false } records a denial: the build is decided, not reported as unreviewed and does not fail strictDepBuilds ("A recorded build the policy denies was decided on purpose"). pnpm 10.26 maps false to ignoredBuiltDependencies, which only silences the report. Under dangerouslyAllowAllBuilds a denied package still builds before pnpm 12.12.0. ↗
  • extra.projectInstall: Commands that install the dependencies of the project: "pnpm install is used to install all dependencies for a project", "Aliases: i". pnpm add (commandLine.add) adds packages. pnpm without a command does not install. ↗
  • extra.commandLine: From cli/add.md, cli/pnx.md, cli/create.md and pnpm-cli.md at that commit. pnx (pnpm 12) has the aliases pnpm dlx and pnpx; pnpm 10 documents pnpm dlx with the alias pnpx (versioned_docs/version-10.x/cli/dlx.md). --shell-mode (-c) runs the command inside a shell, e.g. pnx --package cowsay -c 'echo hi | cowsay'. pnpm create maps foo and create-foo to create-foo, @scope to @scope/create and @scope/foo to @scope/create-foo, and runs it with pnx. --registry and --allow-build are shown with =, the other value options with a separate value. --package belongs to pnx only and is listed there. -F is the short form of --filter (filtering.md). --reporter takes the name of a reporter (cli/install.md) and --loglevel a level (the loglevel setting in settings/cli.md, given as a flag); both take a separate value too. ↗
  • extra.packageSpecs: Sources of pnpm add: the npm registry, jsr:, crate: and pypi: (other registries), workspace packages (the workspace: range protocol, docs/workspaces.md), local directories (./dir is saved as link:, file:./dir is copied), local tarballs ending in .tar, .tar.gz or .tgz, remote tarballs starting with http:// or https://, and git repositories as user/repo (github: by default), github:, bitbucket:, gitlab:, gist: or a full URL such as git+ssh://… or https://github.com/….git. ↗
  • extra.registryOverrides: registry and @scope:registry are read from the command line over .npmrc (docs/npmrc.md in pnpm/pnpm.io). Every pnpm_config_<key> or PNPM_CONFIG_<KEY> variable whose key is a setting sets it (src/env.ts, parseEnvVars), registry included. The user-level .npmrc, which may set any registry, is the file named by --npmrc-auth-file, --userconfig, pnpm_config_npmrc_auth_file, pnpm_config_userconfig, npmrcAuthFile in the global config.yaml or npm_config_userconfig (getNpmrcAuthFile; src/loadNpmrcFiles.ts reads it in place of ~/.npmrc). pnpm 10 reads npm_config_* variables as npm does. ↗
  • extra.parsing: pnpm parses its command line with @pnpm/nopt 0.3.1 (github.com/pnpm/nopt, lib/nopt.js), a fork of nopt: every leading dash is stripped, an exact shorthand or a word of one-letter shorthands is expanded, and an unambiguous prefix of an option of the command is taken for that option, so pnpm add --reg=<url> sets registry; --no-<name> is a negated flag: lib/nopt.js strips every leading no- in any case, each one inverting it, before the prefix is resolved, so --no-ign is --no-ignore-scripts when no other option of the command starts with ign (parse-cli-args, shorthands.ts and pnpm/nopt read again 2026-11-01). Options pnpm does not know make it stop with an error (pnpm11/pnpm/src/main.ts, dropUnknownOptions). --config.<key>=<value> sets the setting <key> (separateConfigDotArgs, normalizeOptions), also in pnpm 10.34.9 (cli/parse-cli-args/src/index.ts). The shorthands are pnpm11/pnpm/src/shorthands.ts; pnpm 10.34.9 has the same without y. ↗
  • extra.booleanWords: The nopt fork pnpm imports (@pnpm/nopt, imported by pnpm11/cli/parse-cli-args/src/index.ts at v12.16.1) reads a Boolean option followed by the word true or false as that value and skips the word, inverted after --no- (parse in lib/nopt.js). ↗
  • extra.directoryOptions: -C <path>, --dir <path>: "Run as if pnpm was started in <path> instead of the current working directory." -C is the shorthand of --dir (parsing.shorthands). pnpm11/cli/parse-cli-args/src/index.ts at v12.16.1 takes dir from the options nopt parsed from the whole command line ("let dir = parsedOpts['dir']") and looks for the workspace from it ("findWorkspaceDir((dir ?? process.cwd()) as string)"). -w, --workspace-root ("Run as if pnpm was started in the root of the workspace") is not listed: it names no directory. ↗
  • extra.configCommands: pnpm config (aliases: c) manages the configuration files: set <key> <value>, get <key>, delete <key> and list; get and list only read. pnpm pkg "Manages the contents of package.json from the command line": get, set, delete and fix, no aliases (docs/cli/pkg.md). ↗
  • extra.installOverrides: pnpm add takes --ignore-scripts ("Same as the ignoreScripts setting") and --allow-build, which lists packages allowed to run postinstall scripts (docs/cli/add.md in pnpm/pnpm.io; a ! prefix denies one instead, still asked about). Any setting can be given as --config.<key>=<value>, the key kept as written (normalizeOptions in pnpm11/cli/parse-cli-args/src/index.ts at v12.16.1), so both spellings are listed and the scanner reads the key without --config.. pnpm_config_<key> and PNPM_CONFIG_<KEY> set the setting of the camel-cased key; a Boolean only from the exact words true and false, a number with Number (parseEnvVars in env.ts). pnpm 10 reads npm_config_* variables as npm does (registryOverrides). minimumReleaseAgeStrict false installs a younger version when no older one matches (user.weakening). ↗
  • extra.minimumReleaseAgeStrict: When false, pnpm falls back to a version that does not meet minimumReleaseAge; when true, resolution fails. It defaults to true when minimumReleaseAge is set explicitly, except in 11.0.0-11.0.3 and 12.0.0-12.2.x (see strictMode): pnpm 11.0.0 and 12.0.0 installed a version 4 hours old with minimumReleaseAge: 4320 on 2026-10-29, and 12.0.0 added it to minimumReleaseAgeExclude. ↗
  • extra.cooldownExactVersions: pnpm 10.17.0: "Don't ignore the minimumReleaseAge check, when the package is requested by exact version". pnpm 10.16.0 installed typescript@7.1.0-dev.20261029.1, 4 hours old, with minimumReleaseAge: 4320 on 2026-10-29. ↗
  • extra.lockfileDocuments: A lockfile whose first line is --- has two YAML documents: a boot document with configDependencies and packageManagerDependencies, written when the project has config dependencies, declares devEngines.packageManager or pins pnpm 12 or newer; then the project lockfile (importers, packages, snapshots), which is always the last document. Seen with pnpm 12.16.1 and packageManager pnpm@12.16.1 on 2026-10-29. ↗
  • extra.lockfileFormat: lockfileVersion 9.0 (pnpm 9 to 12). Registry packages are keyed name@version; git dependencies name@git+<repo>#<commit> with resolution type git, or a tarball from a git host (gitHosted: true; older lockfiles lack the flag, so the host decides); tarball URLs name@https://…; local tarballs name@file:….tgz; local directories have resolution type directory; link: and workspace: dependencies appear only under importers. requiresBuild was removed in lockfile v9, so install scripts are not recorded. A tarball URL under the configured registry is resolved as that registry package and version (resolving/npm-resolver/src/parseBareSpecifier.ts in v10.34.9), so it is keyed name@version and keeps the URL only in the importer specifier; seen in lockfiles written by pnpm 10.34.9 and 12.16.1. ↗
  • extra.npmrcPrecedence: pnpm 10 applies pnpm-workspace.yaml settings over .npmrc and npm_config_* variables; command-line options win over both (same order on v10.16.0). The 10.18.3 changelog says pnpm config set --location=project writes to pnpm-workspace.yaml "matching read priority". The 10.x docs do not state the order. ↗
  • extra.npmrcCooldownKey: pnpm 10 reads minimum-release-age from .npmrc as a number and maps it to minimumReleaseAge (minutes), so the value means the same in both files; pnpm-workspace.yaml wins when both set it (npmrcPrecedence). pnpm 11.0.0 stopped reading it from .npmrc (ignoredSince). ↗
  • extra.sharedWorkspaceLockfile: Same name and default in pnpm 11 and 12, where it is read only from pnpm-workspace.yaml. ↗
  • extra.ignoredSince: Structured form of npmrcIgnored: from 11.0.0 on, other .npmrc settings and the package.json "pnpm" field have no effect. ↗
  • extra.allowAllBuilds: off: the values that leave it off. pnpm tests the value for truth and checks no type: pnpm 10 and 11 read pnpm-workspace.yaml with js-yaml (extra.yamlMergeKeys) and copy each key as it is (getOptionsFromPnpmSettings at 10.28.0, applyWorkspaceSetting at 11.28.10), so a string such as "false", "no" or "true" is on; depcurfew treats every value but false, 0, null and an empty string as on. pnpm 12 reads it into an Option<bool> (dangerously_allow_all_builds in pnpm/crates/config/src/workspace_yaml/settings.rs at 12.16.1) with the YAML 1.1 words of serde-saphyr 0.0.29 (parse_yaml11_bool: true, yes, y, on and false, no, n, off, in any case and also quoted), and stops on any other value, so no value is on there that depcurfew reads as off. Boolean, default false; added in 10.9.0 ("Added a new setting, dangerouslyAllowAllBuilds, for automatically running any scripts of dependencies without the need to approve any builds", pnpm/CHANGELOG.md at v10.26.0). Packages set to false in allowBuilds (or build: false in permissions) stay blocked only from pnpm 12.12.0 (release notes at https://github.com/pnpm/pnpm/releases/tag/v12.12.0: "A package set to false in allowBuilds no longer runs its build scripts when dangerouslyAllowAllBuilds is true", "Previously, dangerouslyAllowAllBuilds ignored the denials"). pnpm 10, 11 and 12.0.0-12.11.x run them too: createAllowBuildFunction returns before it reads the lists ("if (opts.dangerouslyAllowAllBuilds) return () => true" in building/policy at 11.28.10 and builder/policy/src/index.ts at 10.34.9; allow_build_policy.rs at 12.0.0). ↗
  • extra.excludeWildcards: Globs such as @myorg/* in minimumReleaseAgeExclude since 10.17.0. ↗
  • extra.onlyBuiltDependencies: The allow-list before allowBuilds: package.json pnpm.onlyBuiltDependencies, added in 6.32.0 ("If this field exists, only mentioned packages will be able to run install scripts") and working since 6.32.1 ("released with a bug in v6.32.0"); also pnpm-workspace.yaml since 10.5.0 (10.5.0 changelog); removed in 11.0.0 (settings/build.md, see legacyAllowList). Before pnpm 10 any list, even an empty one, lets only the listed packages build (createAllowBuildFunction in @pnpm/builder.policy 3.0.0, which pnpm 9.15.0 uses). ↗
  • extra.ignoredBuiltDependencies: The record of denied builds before allowBuilds (denyList): package.json pnpm.ignoredBuiltDependencies since 10.1.0 ("for explicitly listing packages that should not be built. When a package is in the list, pnpm will not print an info message about that package not being built"), and pnpm-workspace.yaml since 10.5.0 ("Allow to set the "pnpm" settings from package.json via the pnpm-workspace.yaml file"). The 10.26.0 entry for allowBuilds says that allowBuilds: { core-js: false } "achieves the same result as" ignoredBuiltDependencies: [core-js]. It only silences the report: createAllowBuildFunction (builder/policy/src/index.ts at v10.25.0) does not read it, so a package onlyBuiltDependencies names still builds. Deprecated in 10.26.0 and removed in 11.0.0 (see legacyAllowList). ↗
  • extra.npmrcBuildSettings: pnpm 10 reads dangerously-allow-all-builds from the project .npmrc as from every .npmrc, with parseField of @pnpm/npm-conf, so every value but false, null and undefined is on (user.weakening). From 10.13.0 config/config/src/index.ts takes the build settings of all .npmrc files and npm_config_* variables aside (extractAndRemoveDependencyBuildOptions) and applies them only when pnpm-workspace.yaml and the "pnpm" field of package.json (from which getOptionsFromRootManifest takes onlyBuiltDependencies, onlyBuiltDependenciesFile and neverBuiltDependencies) set none of projectKeys (hasDependencyBuildOptions: a key that is not null or undefined); the list gained allowBuilds in 10.26.0 (dependencyBuildOptions.ts at 10.13.0, 10.25.0, 10.26.0 and 10.28.0; the file does not exist in 10.12.1). Before 10.13.0 the .npmrc value applies unless pnpm-workspace.yaml sets dangerouslyAllowAllBuilds itself (npmrcPrecedence). pnpm 11 reads only auth and registry settings from .npmrc (ignoredSince). ↗
  • extra.yamlMergeKeys: pnpm-workspace.yaml and the global config.yaml (readWorkspaceManifest with cfgFileName) are read with read-yaml-file, which calls load of js-yaml with its default schema, and that schema has the merge type: read-yaml-file 3.0.0 with @zkochan/js-yaml 0.0.11 in 11.28.10, read-yaml-file 2.1.0 with @zkochan/js-yaml 0.0.9 in 10.28.0 and 0.0.11 in 10.34.9 (workspace/read-manifest/src/index.ts and pnpm-lock.yaml at those tags; lib/schema/default.js of the packages). pnpm 12 reads both files with serde_saphyr::from_str (pnpm/crates/config/src/workspace_yaml/parse_settings.rs at 12.16.1), whose default MergeKeyPolicy is Merge (serde-saphyr 0.0.29). So <<: *defaults brings the keys of the anchored map into the map, and settings set that way apply (a review run with pnpm 10.28.0: pnpm config get dangerouslyAllowAllBuilds gave true and installs ran scripts). A key written in the map itself wins over a merged one whatever their order (mergeMappings of js-yaml adds only missing keys; serde-saphyr adds merged entries whose key is not seen), and earlier maps of <<: [*a, *b] win over later ones; a quoted "<<" is an ordinary key. ↗
  • extra.latestVersion: pnpm 12.0.0 shipped on 26 Aug and 11.0.0 on 28 Apr (GitHub shows no year; 2026 inferred from release order). 11.x and 10.x still receive patches. ↗
  • extra.buildPermissions: permissions.<package>.build in pnpm-workspace.yaml: true lets the package run its build scripts, false denies them; the keys take the same forms as those of allowBuilds, and the entry outranks the package's allowBuilds entry (decided_build_approvals and apply_permissions in pnpm/crates/config/src/workspace_yaml/permissions.rs at v12.16.1, which warns when a package is in both). A string is an undecided placeholder (AllowBuild::Undecided in sections.rs). Added in 12.11.0 (docs/settings/build.md and docs/cli/permissions.md); pnpm approve writes a build decision there only when the file already has permissions. ↗
  • extra.strictDepBuilds: When true, an unreviewed dependency build script fails the install. ↗
  • extra.strictMode: In non-strict mode pnpm silently writes immature picks into minimumReleaseAgeExclude. Strict mode fails CI installs with ERR_PNPM_NO_MATURE_MATCHING_VERSION. ↗

Yarn (Berry)

  • cooldown.configFiles: Also YARN_NPM_MINIMAL_AGE_GATE (general YARN_ env rule) and, since 4.17.0, npmScopes.<scope>.npmMinimalAgeGate. package.json cannot set it. ↗
  • cooldown.unit: Bare numbers are minutes. Since 4.11.0 duration strings with ms, s, m, h, d or w (e.g. 3d, 1w) also work; on 4.10.x a string such as 7d silently had no effect. ↗
  • cooldown.availableSince: The setting arrived in 4.10.0 (release notes, pull request and source; the security docs page at https://yarnpkg.com/features/security says 4.12). 4.10.2 fixed it: "Bust the metadata cache" (#6908) and "support registries without publish times using minimalAgeGate" (#6907). On 2026-10-29 Yarn 4.10.1 with npmMinimalAgeGate: 4320 refused zod@3.23.8 and esbuild@0.25.0, both over a year old ("No candidates found"), while 4.10.2, 4.10.3, 4.11.0 and 4.12.0 installed them and refused a version 4 hours old. depcurfew writes the setting from 4.10.2 on. ↗
  • installScripts.defaultPolicy: Workspace scripts still run. Upgraded projects with an old lockfile get enableScripts: true written to .yarnrc.yml. Yarn 1 runs scripts unless --ignore-scripts is passed. ↗
  • installScripts.allowList.file: Root package.json only; ignored inside workspaces. ↗
  • installScripts.disableAll: Set in .yarnrc.yml; the default since 4.14.0. yarn install --mode=skip-build skips builds for one run. Berry ignores YARN_IGNORE_SCRIPTS. ↗
  • sources.git.default: Upgraded projects with an old lockfile get ["**"] written to .yarnrc.yml. ↗
  • project.lockfiles: Yarn Classic uses the same name; see berryLockfileMarker and classicLockfileHeader to tell them apart. ↗
  • project.workspaces: Array of folder glob patterns. ↗
  • user.files: Configuration.find adds .yarnrc.yml from the home folder (folderUtils.getHomeFolder: os.homedir(), else /usr/local/share) with the lowest priority, also for projects outside the home folder; the name stays .yarnrc.yml even when YARN_RC_FILENAME is set. The yarnrc page does not describe it; yarn config set -H writes the home file (https://yarnpkg.com/cli/config/set). The lookup is the same in 4.0.0, and the 2.2.0 changelog already speaks of the home configuration file; the first version was not tracked. ↗
  • user.settings: Configuration.use knows no project-only settings, so the home file sets these wherever the version has them; the first versions are those of cooldown, cooldown.exclude and sources.git, and enableScripts is checked in 4.0.0. enableScripts: false denies every dependency build script (scriptsSwitch); buildsDeny is the closest purpose, since Yarn has no strict mode. The allow-list dependenciesMeta.<package>.built is read from the root package.json only (Project.getDependencyMeta), so Yarn has no user-level allow-list. depcurfew does not write npmScopes.<scope>.npmMinimalAgeGate. ↗
  • user.unknownKeys: Configuration.use sets a key it does not know aside without an error when it comes from the home file, even with enableStrictSettings; other files throw. The 2.2.0 changelog: "Unsupported options will simply be silently ignored." ↗
  • extra.registrySettings: npmRegistryServer: "Define the registry to use when fetching packages"; npmScopes: "Per-scope registry configurations", whose entries take npmRegistryServer ("See npmRegistryServer"), keyed by the scope without @. "While Yarn will automatically find them in the parent directories, they should usually be kept at the root of your project". Yarn (Berry) does not read registry= from .npmrc. ↗
  • extra.yamlMergeKeys: parseSyml reads .yarnrc.yml with load of js-yaml and FAILSAFE_SCHEMA, which has no merge type (only strings, sequences and maps), so <<: *defaults is an ordinary key named <<: Configuration.use rejects it as an unrecognized setting in a project .yarnrc.yml (enableStrictSettings, default true) and sets it aside in the home file, so merged values never apply. Every scalar is a string to Yarn, which parses it per setting (parseValue in packages/yarnpkg-core/sources/Configuration.ts). ↗
  • extra.classicUserFile: Yarn Classic (1.x, last release 1.22.22) reads .yarnrc and .npmrc from the project, its parent folders, the home folder (homeConfigLoc = ~/.yarnrc) and the global prefix (getPossibleConfigLocations in src/registries/npm-registry.js), never .yarnrc.yml, which only Yarn 2 and later read. depcurfew does not configure Yarn Classic. ↗
  • extra.denyList: With built: false in the root package.json, Yarn reports that the build "has been explicitly disabled through configuration" as an info message instead of the warning that all build scripts are disabled; built: false also wins over enableScripts: true. Only the top-level manifest counts (CHANGELOG 2.0.0). ↗
  • extra.projectInstall: The install command ("install the project dependencies") has the paths [install] and Command.Default, so yarn without a command installs too. yarn add (commandLine.add) adds packages. ↗
  • extra.commandLine: dlx.ts (documented at https://yarnpkg.com/cli/dlx) downloads the package named by the command, or the ones given with -p/--package, into a temporary environment and runs it. create.ts (yarn create, not on yarnpkg.com) takes -p/--package as well and runs create-<name>: @foo becomes @foo/create, @foo/app becomes @foo/create-app, foo becomes create-foo, and a name that already matches ^create(-|$) is kept (so is create alone). yarn add (https://yarnpkg.com/cli/add) takes packages as arguments; of its options only --mode takes a value. yarn workspace <workspaceName> <commandName> … runs a command within that workspace, e.g. yarn workspace components add -D react (https://yarnpkg.com/cli/workspace). ↗
  • extra.packageSpecs: Protocols besides npm: and git: exec:, file:, jsr:, link:, patch:, portal: and workspace:. link: connects an external directory (https://yarnpkg.com/protocol/link), portal: a relative path to a package folder (https://yarnpkg.com/protocol/portal) and file: copies a folder or tarball. ↗
  • extra.registryOverrides: Environment variables that start with yarn_ (any case) set the setting named by the camel-cased rest, so YARN_NPM_REGISTRY_SERVER sets npmRegistryServer, the registry packages are fetched from (https://yarnpkg.com/configuration/yarnrc#npmRegistryServer). getRcFilename reads yarn_rc_filename (any case) as the name of the configuration file instead of .yarnrc.yml. npmScopes, an object, cannot be set this way. ↗
  • extra.lockfileFormat: Each entry has resolution: name@<locator>: npm:<version> for the registry, <repository>#commit=<sha> for git (GitHub shortcuts become https://github.com/<owner>/<repo>.git), an http(s) URL for tarballs, file:<path> (a .tgz or .tar.gz path is a tarball, anything else a directory), portal: and link: for directories, workspace: for workspace packages, and patch: around the URL-encoded original locator. No field records install scripts; dependenciesMeta.built: false is a user opt-out. ↗
  • extra.durationStrings: npmMinimalAgeGate accepts a number followed by ms, s, m, h, d or w (e.g. 3d, 1w) since 4.11.0; on 4.10.x such a string silently had no effect. ↗
  • extra.excludeWildcards: Name globs such as @scope/* or package* in npmPreapprovedPackages. ↗
  • extra.classicLockfileHeader: Written as # yarn lockfile v${LOCKFILE_VERSION} with LOCKFILE_VERSION = 1 (src/constants.js). ↗
  • extra.latestVersion: Yarn 6 is still in preview. ↗
  • extra.directoryOptions: checkCwd takes --cwd <dir> or --cwd=<dir> only as the first argument ("argv[0] === --cwd", "argv[0].startsWith(--cwd=)"), and for yarn add also as the last two words ("argv[0] === add && argv[argv.length - 2] === --cwd"; "CRA adds --cwd at the end of the command"), removes them and runs the command with cli.defaultContext.cwd = ppath.resolve(cwd). Yarn finds the project and its .yarnrc.yml files from that directory. ↗
  • extra.configCommands: plugin-essentials registers config get, config set and config unset next to config (commands/config/*.ts at 4.18.1). yarn config set updates the project configuration, or the home configuration with -H/--home (commands/config/set.ts); yarn config and yarn config get only print settings (commands/config.ts: "This command prints the current active configuration settings"). ↗
  • extra.installOverrides: yarn add declares noTimeGate = Option.Boolean(--no-time-gate), "Disable the minimum release age check for this command" (plugin-essentials/sources/commands/add.ts at 4.18.1). getEnvironmentSettings lowercases every environment variable, keeps those starting with yarn_ and camel-cases the rest into a setting (ENVIRONMENT_PREFIX), so YARN_NPM_MINIMAL_AGE_GATE sets npmMinimalAgeGate, YARN_NPM_PREAPPROVED_PACKAGES npmPreapprovedPackages and YARN_ENABLE_SCRIPTS enableScripts; the values that keep scripts off are those of user.variables. ↗
  • extra.perScopeGate: getMinimalAgeGate: for a scoped package, a number in npmScopes.<scope>.npmMinimalAgeGate (the scope name without @) replaces the global gate, longer or shorter, and 0 turns the gate off for that scope (acceptance tests "npmScopes override" in features/npmMinimalAgeGate.test.ts at 4.18.1; the code is the same in 4.17.0 and absent in 4.16.0). Each npmScopes entry is a shape whose unset properties get their defaults (parseShape and getDefaultValue in yarnpkg-core Configuration.ts), so a scope listed only for its registry gets the default of 1d (scopablePackageGateSettings in plugin-npm index.ts) instead of the global gate; read from the code, no test covers that case. ↗
  • extra.upgradeMigration: Explicit values written by depcurfew prevent the migration. ↗

Bun

  • cooldown.configFiles: Also the global $HOME/.bunfig.toml or $XDG_CONFIG_HOME/.bunfig.toml; local keys override global ones. Bun's .npmrc support has no min-release-age key. ↗
  • cooldown.cliFlag: bun install, add and update; bunx honors it only since 1.4.4. ↗
  • cooldown.unit: Plain numbers are seconds; 0 turns it off. Since 1.4.4 duration strings such as 90m, 12 hours, 3d or 2 weeks also work (the bunfig reference still documents seconds only). ↗
  • cooldown.exclude.patternSyntax: Since 1.4.4 --minimum-release-age-excludes replaces the bunfig list for one command. ↗
  • installScripts.defaultPolicy: Dependency scripts run only for Bun's built-in trusted list (npm-registry packages only since 1.4) or trustedDependencies; the root project's scripts run. First version of this policy not tracked. ↗
  • installScripts.allowList.setting: Replaces the built-in default list rather than extending it. bun pm trust and bun add --trust write it. ↗
  • installScripts.disableAll: Default false. Also --ignore-scripts or ignore-scripts=true in .npmrc. Skips the project's own scripts and trustedDependencies too. ↗
  • project.workspaces: Full glob syntax, including ! patterns. ↗
  • project.workspacesObjectKey: The object form also holds catalog and catalogs. ↗
  • user.files: "When XDG_CONFIG_HOME is set to an absolute path, Bun reads the file there and not the one in $HOME"; HOME is USERPROFILE on Windows (src/bun_core/env_var.rs). Only one file is read, with no fallback when the XDG one is missing (get_home_config_path in src/bunfig/arguments.rs). Only package manager commands read it (bun install, add, remove, update, pm, bunx and so on), before the project bunfig.toml, whose keys win. 1.2.0 already reads it, and the first version was not tracked; 1.2.0 and 1.3.0 used XDG_CONFIG_HOME whenever it was set, and the version that started requiring an absolute path was not pinned. ↗
  • user.settings: parse_install reads both from the user file as from bunfig.toml (only globalDir and globalBinDir are user-only), as in 1.3.0 (bunfig.zig). trustedDependencies exists only in package.json and bunfig has no such key, so Bun has no user-level allow-list; install.ignoreScripts only turns scripts off. Write whole seconds: duration strings need 1.4.4. ↗
  • user.unknownKeys: The parser looks up only the keys it knows (install_obj.get), so another key, such as install.minimumReleaseAge before 1.3.0, is neither read nor reported. ↗
  • user.invalidValueFails: load_global_bunfig ends the process through report_bunfig_load_failure when the user file has a value Bun cannot read, e.g. minimumReleaseAge = "3d" before 1.4.4 ("Expected number of seconds for minimumReleaseAge"), so every package manager command fails; 1.3.0 crashed the same way. A file Bun cannot read at all is skipped. ↗
  • user.weakening: Both files fill one set of options, the user file first, so a key in the project bunfig.toml replaces the user value; an empty minimumReleaseAgeExcludes list is skipped, so a project's [] does not clear the user list. 0 turns the delay off (src/install/PackageManager/PackageManagerOptions.rs keeps only values above 0). The command line beats both files. ↗
  • extra.registrySettings: "Put bunfig.toml in your project root, alongside your package.json"; the global $HOME/.bunfig.toml (or $XDG_CONFIG_HOME/.bunfig.toml) is loaded too, and local keys win. "The default registry is https://registry.npmjs.org/. To change it": [install] registry, a string or { url, token }. "To configure a registry for a particular scope (for example, @myorg/<package>), use install.scopes": a key per scope (myorg = "https://registry.myorg.com/" or a table with url). ↗
  • extra.projectInstall: "if your project has a package.json, you can use bun install"; the page names no alias, so none is listed. bun add (commandLine.add) adds packages. ↗
  • extra.commandLine: Options of bun add (snippets/cli/add.mdx) and bunx (snippets/cli/bunx.mdx) typed string or number; --catalog takes its name only after =. -p is --package for bunx but the flag --production for bun add, so it is listed with bunx only. The short forms -F (--filter) and -c (--config) of bun add are left out because bunx does not document them, and an option wrongly read as taking a value would hide the package after it. bunx is an alias for bun x (docs/pm/bunx.mdx). bun create <template> runs the create-<template> package from npm unless a local template has that name; it also takes GitHub repositories and local paths (docs/runtime/templating/create.mdx). ↗
  • extra.packageSpecs: Git dependencies: "Bun supports a variety of protocols, including github, git, git+ssh, and git+https", with the examples git@github.com:moment/moment.git and github:colinhacks/zod. Tarball URLs, local folders and local .tgz files are accepted too. ↗
  • extra.registryOverrides: bun add takes --registry and --config (-c), a configuration file (snippets/cli/add.mdx). BUN_CONFIG_REGISTRY sets the npm registry and environment variables take priority over bunfig.toml (pm/cli/install.mdx). ↗
  • extra.lockfileFormat: bun.lock (text, the default since 1.2) maps each package to a tuple starting with name@<spec>: name@<version> followed by the registry for npm, name@git+<repo>#<sha> and name@github:<owner>/<repo>#<sha> for git, name@https://… for tarball URLs, name@./x.tgz (without file:) for local tarballs, name@file:<dir> and name@link:<dir> for directories, name@workspace:<dir> for workspace packages. The info object carries hasInstallScript: true only since 1.4.4, with no lockfileVersion change, so a missing flag proves nothing for older files. bun.lockb is an undocumented binary format. ↗
  • extra.durationStrings: Since 1.4.4 minimumReleaseAge also takes duration strings such as 90m, 12 hours, 3d or 2 weeks; plain numbers stay seconds. ↗
  • extra.excludeWildcards: minimumReleaseAgeExcludes takes exact package names (and name@version since 1.4.4), no wildcards. ↗
  • extra.trustedDependenciesReplacesDefaults: So an empty list trusts no package: without the field Bun 1.4.4 ran esbuild's postinstall (esbuild is on the built-in list); with "trustedDependencies": [] it blocked it (bun pm untrusted listed it), tested on 2026-10-29. ↗
  • extra.directoryOptions: bun add: <ParamField path="--cwd" type="string"> "Set a specific current working directory" (Global Configuration & Context); bun install has the same option, "Set a specific cwd" (docs/snippets/cli/install.mdx at the same tag, General Configuration). ↗
  • extra.installOverrides: bun add @types/bun --minimum-release-age 259200 # seconds: "A number is in seconds", or a number with a unit such as 90m, 12 hours, 3d or 2 weeks (durationStrings). The page lists no environment variable for it. bun add --trust "adds the package to trustedDependencies in the project's package.json and installs it", so its lifecycle scripts run (docs/snippets/cli/add.mdx and docs/pm/lifecycle.mdx at bun-v1.4.4); lifecycle.mdx names no variable that turns scripts on, and ignore-scripts read from the environment "only turns scripts off". ↗

Deno

  • cooldown.setting: A number or string, or the object form { "age": ..., "exclude": [...] }. Covers npm and JSR packages. ↗
  • cooldown.configFiles: min-release-age in .npmrc since 2.8; also NPM_CONFIG_MIN_RELEASE_AGE. This page does not state precedence; denoland/deno#35458 gives CLI flag, deno.json, .npmrc, environment, built-in default. ↗
  • cooldown.cliFlag: Alias --min-dep-age since 2.9.3. deno install honors it since 2.5.5; deno add, deno remove and deno x since 2.9.4. ↗
  • cooldown.unit: Numbers are minutes; strings may be an ISO-8601 duration (P3D, PT72H), an absolute date or an RFC3339 timestamp; 0 turns it off. The .npmrc format differs (see npmrcFormat). ↗
  • cooldown.default: Since 2.9 a 24-hour minimum applies when nothing is configured; set 0 to turn it off. Whether the default covers JSR is not stated. ↗
  • cooldown.exclude.patternSyntax: Before 2.9.2 wildcard entries were stored literally and never matched. No CLI flag or .npmrc key for exclusions. ↗
  • installScripts.defaultPolicy: npm lifecycle scripts run only when approved, and only with a node_modules directory. deno x / dx runs them after its download prompt is accepted. ↗
  • installScripts.allowList.setting: deno approve-scripts writes it. --allow-scripts=npm:pkg on the CLI exists since 1.45. ↗
  • installScripts.allowList.file: Must be set at the workspace root. ↗
  • sources.git.setting: git+https: and git+ssh: dependencies are not supported in package.json. ↗
  • sources.remoteTarball.setting: Tarball URL dependencies are not supported in package.json. ↗
  • sources.file.setting: No setting. Since 2.8 file: and link: entries are silently skipped (scope disputed, see fileLinkSkipped); deno.json "links" overrides dependencies with local packages. ↗
  • sources.directory.setting: No setting; local directories behave like file: and link: entries (see fileLinkSkipped). ↗
  • project.lockfiles: The default location; lock.path in deno.json can move it and "lock": false turns it off. ↗
  • project.workspaces: Paths of members; globs and ! exclusions since Deno 2.1. ↗
  • user.files: Deno has no user configuration file of its own (deno.json is looked up from the working directory upwards). It reads .npmrc next to package.json or deno.json and in the home directory (HOME, USERPROFILE on Windows), not the file named by NPM_CONFIG_USERCONFIG and not under XDG_CONFIG_HOME. Home discovery arrived in 1.45.0 (#24021); since 2.1.8 (#27119) the project and home files are merged key by key, before that a project .npmrc hid the home one (Releases.md). This is the ~/.npmrc that npm reads. A home .npmrc Deno may not read is skipped; one that does not parse stops the command. ↗
  • user.settings: 2.8.0: "feat(npmrc): support min-release-age (#33983)". A whole number of days (cooldownUnit); the same line sets npm's min-release-age, also in days. minimumDependencyAge.exclude and allowScripts exist only in deno.json (allowScripts at the workspace root), so Deno has no user-level exclusions or script settings. ↗
  • user.cooldownUnit: NpmRc::parse reads min-release-age "in days" (min_release_age_days: Option<u64>; "npm interprets the value as a number of days"): an INI number of 0 or more, or a text that parses as a whole number after ${VAR} expansion; anything else, such as 72h or 1.5, is ignored without a message, so the age comes from the next source. The project .npmrc wins over the home file (libs/resolver/npmrc.rs), and the days count only where deno.json and --min-dep-age set nothing; those take minutes, an ISO-8601 duration or an RFC 3339 date (libs/config/util.rs, libs/cli_parser/src/defs.rs). ↗
  • user.unknownKeys: NpmRc::parse reads registry, auth, replace-registry-host, min-release-age and trust-policy keys and skips every other key without a message. ↗
  • user.invalidValueFails: A min-release-age that is not a whole number of days, such as 72h, is ignored without a message (test "invalid value is ignored"), so the age silently comes from the next source. A home .npmrc that does not parse at all stops the command (libs/resolver/npmrc.rs). ↗
  • user.weakening: The age comes from the command line, then minimumDependencyAge in deno.json, then .npmrc (the project file over the home file), then NPM_CONFIG_MIN_RELEASE_AGE, then the built-in 1440 minutes; 0 from .npmrc turns it off (apply_minimum_dependency_age_fallbacks). The same line turns npm's delay off too. ↗
  • extra.denyList: The object form "allowScripts": { "allow": [...], "deny": ["npm:pkg"] } (schema since 2.6.0: "List of npm package constraints to deny running lifecycle scripts for"); deny beats allow, and a denied package is not added to the packages whose scripts were not run, so Deno does not warn about it. ↗
  • extra.commandLine: install.md: deno install [PACKAGES] installs packages and adds them to deno.json or package.json, deno add is its alias, deno install --global installs a package or script as a binary, and deno install --entrypoint (-e) [FILES] installs what the files use. -n/--name, --root, --os and --arch take a value; --allow-scripts and --node-modules-dir are shown with =. Since Deno 2.8 a name without a prefix is an npm package; JSR packages need jsr: (add.md). x.md: deno x runs a package from npm or JSR without installing it, like npx (deno x npm:create-vite my-app); --package (-p) names the package when the binary differs (deno x -p typescript tsc), and npm:<package>/<binary> picks one binary; deno x --install-alias creates the dx alias. In Deno 2.9.8 (github.com/denoland/deno, cli/args/flags.rs) an executable whose name ends in dx, denox or dnx runs as deno x, and libs/cli_parser/src/defs.rs (X_SUBCOMMAND with COMPILE_ARGS, PERMISSION_ARGS, RUNTIME_MISC_ARGS and INSPECT_ARGS) gives the options of deno x that take a separate value; the permission flags take theirs after =. -L/--log-level is a global option with a value. ↗
  • extra.lockfileFormat: deno.lock version 5 (the default since Deno 2.3) marks npm packages with lifecycle scripts as scripts: true (hasInstallScript from the registry or a preinstall, install or postinstall script, libs/npm/resolution/graph.rs); version 4 has no such field. tarball is written only for registries other than the default one. Deno rejects git and http(s) dependencies in package.json and keeps file: dependencies out of the lockfile. ↗
  • extra.durationStrings: Strings are ISO-8601 durations (P3D, PT72H), an absolute date or an RFC3339 timestamp; the last two set a fixed cut-off instead of a delay. ↗
  • extra.ageObjectKey: In the object form of minimumDependencyAge, age holds the delay and exclude the exemptions. ↗
  • extra.excludeWildcards: A trailing * matches by prefix (npm:@scope/*) since 2.9.2; earlier wildcard entries never matched. ↗
  • extra.npmSpecifierPrefix: Exclusions and allowScripts entries name npm packages as npm:<name>. ↗
  • extra.allowAllScripts: allowScripts: true allows every npm lifecycle script. Only the JSON value true does: to_allow_scripts_config (libs/config/deno_json/mod.rs at v2.9.8) gives every package for true, none for false and null, and stops with "expected string array, boolean, or object" for a string or a number, so "true" or 1 cannot turn it on silently. ↗
  • extra.installOverrides: deno install --minimum-dependency-age=P3D: deno.json and the flag accept an ISO-8601 duration such as P3D or PT72H, or an integer of minutes, and 0 turns it off; --min-dep-age is its alias (cooldown.cliFlag). deno install --allow-scripts=<packages> lets those packages run their lifecycle scripts, which Deno does not run by default (runtime/reference/cli/install.md in denoland/docs). ↗

uv

  • cooldown.setting: Limits candidate packages to those uploaded before a date or, since 0.9.17, a duration ago. Compared with the upload time of each file, not the release date. false turns off a value from a lower-priority source (since 0.11.24). ↗
  • cooldown.configFiles: Top level of uv.toml or the [tool.uv] table of pyproject.toml. uv searches the current directory and its parents and uses the first directory with uv.toml or a pyproject.toml that has [tool.uv]; uv.toml wins over [tool.uv] in the same directory, with a warning. In a workspace only the root is read. User (~/.config/uv/uv.toml) and system (/etc/uv/uv.toml) files are merged below the project file; uv tool and uvx read only those. UV_EXCLUDE_NEWER overrides the files. ↗
  • cooldown.cliFlag: Also the UV_EXCLUDE_NEWER environment variable. ↗
  • cooldown.unit: A "friendly" duration such as 24 hours, 1 week or 30 days, or an ISO 8601 duration such as P7D; months and years are rejected, a day is always 24 hours. An RFC 3339 timestamp is a fixed cutoff instead of a rolling delay. ↗
  • cooldown.availableSince: Durations ("dependency cooldowns") since 0.9.17; before that exclude-newer took only dates, which do not move with time. Tested on 2026-10-30: uv 0.9.16 printed a warning that "100000 days" is not a date and resolved with no delay at all; 0.9.17 and later held back every version. ↗
  • cooldown.default: No default: without a value every upload time is accepted. ↗
  • cooldown.exclude.setting: A table mapping package names to false (exempt), a date or a duration; package values take precedence over the global and index values. ↗
  • cooldown.exclude.availableSince: The table exists since 0.8.4 (dates); false, which exempts a package, since 0.9.25. Tested on 2026-10-30: with a false entry, uv 0.9.17 warned "data did not match any variant of untagged enum" and ignored the whole [tool.uv] table, the delay included; 0.9.25 and 0.13.6 exempted the package. ↗
  • cooldown.exclude.patternSyntax: Keys are package names, compared after name normalization. ↗
  • installScripts.defaultPolicy: no-build defaults to false: source distributions, git and directory dependencies are built, which runs their build backend. Installing a wheel runs no package code. ↗
  • installScripts.allowList.setting: With no-build = true, the packages in no-binary-package are the only ones built from source ("Allow all to be overridden by specific binary exclusions"). Their wheels are not used, so they are always built. ↗
  • installScripts.allowList.file: Or uv.toml; the same table as exclude-newer. ↗
  • installScripts.allowList.availableSince: Project settings no-build, no-build-package, no-binary and no-binary-package; uv sync and related commands respect them since 0.4.6. ↗
  • installScripts.disableAll: Operations that need to build a source distribution fail. Workspace members, editable requirements and wheels built earlier and kept in the cache are still used. With uv 0.13.6 on 2026-10-30, uv sync refused a git dependency and a non-editable local directory dependency as well ("marked as --no-build but has no binary distribution"), so those need an exception too. An explicit no-build = false in a higher-priority file makes uv ignore no-build-package. ↗
  • project.lockfiles: Created next to pyproject.toml. ↗
  • project.workspaces: The [tool.uv.workspace] table of the root pyproject.toml lists members and exclude (globs or paths); exclude wins over members, and the root is a member too. ↗
  • project.workspacesObjectKey: The workspace guide calls members required, while the settings reference (crates/uv-workspace/src/pyproject.rs) gives it a default of []. ↗
  • user.files: User-level configuration is <config dir>/uv/uv.toml: $XDG_CONFIG_HOME/uv, else ~/.config/uv on Linux and macOS (not ~/Library), %APPDATA%\uv, else uv in the Roaming AppData known folder, on Windows (FilesystemOptions::user in crates/uv-settings/src/lib.rs). The directory comes from the etcetera crate (crates/uv-dirs/src/lib.rs), which uses XDG_CONFIG_HOME only when it is an absolute path. Keys sit at the top level, as in [tool.uv] without the prefix; "User- and system-level configuration files cannot use the pyproject.toml format" (docs/concepts/configuration-files.md).
