The system file (/etc/uv/uv.toml, %PROGRAMDATA%\uv\uv.toml) is merged below it. Same path code at 0.9.0, 0.9.16 and 0.13.6; earlier versions are not tracked. ↗
  • user.settings: Settings of uv.toml are merged with project-level configuration taking precedence over the user-level configuration: a project exclude-newer or no-build wins, exclude-newer-package tables are merged with the project keys winning, and lists such as no-build-package and no-binary-package are concatenated (crates/uv-settings/src/combine.rs). uv tool and uvx read only the user and system files, so the user file is the only file that gives them these settings; uv pip applies the top-level keys unless its [pip] table overrides them. The versions are those of the values depcurfew writes: a duration in exclude-newer since 0.9.17, package = false in exclude-newer-package since 0.9.25. no-build-package takes effect only while no-build is unset. ↗
  • user.unknownKeys: uv.toml is parsed with deny_unknown_fields (OptionsWire), and fields allowed only in pyproject.toml (workspace, sources, dev-dependencies, conflicts, managed, package and others) are rejected by validate_uv_toml (crates/uv-settings/src/lib.rs, Error::PyprojectOnlyField); both are errors of the user file (see invalidValueFails). ↗
  • user.invalidValueFails: FilesystemOptions::user() returns nothing only for a missing, not-a-directory or unreadable file; a TOML or schema error (Error::UvToml) is passed on, and crates/uv/src/lib.rs stops every command that reads configuration with it, unlike an invalid pyproject.toml, which is only warned about. So a value the installed uv does not know (a duration before 0.9.17, package = false before 0.9.25, exclude-newer = false before 0.11.24) breaks every uv command instead of being dropped. A required-version mismatch is reported before a schema error. ↗
  • user.weakening: Tables are merged with the project keys winning, lists are extended and scalars come from the project when it sets them (combine.rs); with no-build = false, NoBuild::from_args ignores the package list (crates/uv-configuration/src/build_options.rs). exclude-newer = false (since 0.11.24) turns off a value from a lower-priority source, the system file included. Read from the code, not run: a [pip] exclude-newer in the user file is combined above the top-level keys for uv pip, so it can override a project exclude-newer there, and a user [[index]] with exclude-newer = false (preview, 0.11.5) exempts the packages of that index. The [pip] table configures "just the uv pip command line interface", and its settings win over the top-level ones there ("many of the settings in this section have corollaries in the top-level namespace which do apply to the uv pip interface unless they are overridden by a value in the uv.pip section", docs/concepts/configuration-files.md); PipOptions has exclude-newer (false disables it), exclude-newer-package, no-build and no-binary (a list, :all: for every package), and the top level no-binary = true builds every package from source (settings.rs). [pip] no-build = false weakens nothing: PipSettings::combine passes it to NoBuild::from_pip_args, which gives NoBuild::None (or the [pip] only-binary list) for false, and combines that with the top-level no-build and no-build-package (NoBuild::combine: All wins, None gives the other side), so it cannot turn the top-level no-build or no-build-package off; a project [tool.uv.pip] no-build wins over the user value anyway (crates/uv-cli/src/settings.rs and crates/uv-configuration/src/build_options.rs at 0.13.6, the same in crates/uv/src/settings.rs at 0.9.17). ↗
  • user.variables: "Settings provided via environment variables take precedence over persistent configuration, and settings provided via the command line take precedence over both" (docs/concepts/configuration-files.md). UV_NO_BUILD_PACKAGE since 0.6.5, UV_NO_BINARY and UV_NO_BINARY_PACKAGE since 0.5.30 (lists separated by spaces), UV_NO_CONFIG since 0.2.30, UV_CONFIG_FILE since 0.1.34. UV_NO_BUILD, UV_NO_BINARY and UV_NO_CONFIG are flags parsed with the BoolishValueParser of clap (crates/uv-cli/src/lib.rs), whose true values are y, yes, t, true, on and 1 and false values n, no, f, false, off and 0, in any case (https://github.com/clap-rs/clap/blob/v4.6.7/clap_builder/src/util/str_to_bool.rs, the clap_builder version in the Cargo.lock of uv); a false value leaves the flag unset (flag() in crates/uv-cli/src/options.rs gives None), so the files decide (with UV_NO_BUILD=0 uv 0.11.32 still refused to build in a review test on 2026-10-31). XDG_CONFIG_HOME and APPDATA move the user directory (see files). ↗
  • extra.registrySettings: ResolverInstallerSchema: index ("The package indexes to use when resolving dependencies", [[tool.uv.index]] with name and url), index-url ("The URL of the Python package index (by default: https://pypi.org/simple)"), extra-index-url ("Extra URLs of package indexes to use, in addition to --index-url") and find-links ("Locations to search for candidate distributions, in addition to those found in the registry indexes"); PipOptions ([tool.uv.pip]) has index-url, extra-index-url and find-links too. uv.toml takes the same keys at its top level (extra.configTables). docs/concepts/indexes.md: "Indexes are prioritized in the order in which they're defined", the default index is "always treated as lowest priority", with the default first-index strategy uv "will stop at the first index on which a given package is available", default = true replaces PyPI ("set default = true on another index entry" to "exclude PyPI from the list of indexes"), so an entry without it adds an index that is asked before the default one, and an explicit = true index is used only for packages pinned to it in tool.uv.sources. Each [[index]] entry is read as entries says (decision D-120): explicit = true counts only for the packages tool.uv.sources pins to it by name, default = true replaces PyPI, any other entry adds an index (see INDEX_ENTRIES). ↗
  • extra.pylockCreatedBy: uv export --format pylock.toml (and uv pip compile to pylock.toml) writes created-by = "uv". The project itself and workspace members are written as directory = { path = …, editable = true }, with the path relative to the lock file, "." for the project. ↗
  • extra.configTables: Settings sit in [tool.uv] in pyproject.toml and at the top level of uv.toml. uv.toml cannot hold workspace, sources and a few other project fields. ↗
  • extra.pyprojectTable: A pyproject.toml without a [tool.uv] table is ignored when uv searches for configuration. ↗
  • extra.configPrecedence: A nested pyproject.toml with any [tool.uv] table hides the configuration of its parent directories. ↗
  • extra.configFileOrder: "configuration will be read from uv.toml, and the [tool.uv] section in the accompanying pyproject.toml will be ignored"; uv prints a warning listing the ignored fields. ↗
  • extra.workspaceRootOnly: "In workspaces, uv will begin its search at the workspace root, ignoring any configuration defined in workspace members." Project fields such as tool.uv.sources of a member still apply. ↗
  • extra.buildSwitch: The project setting that forbids building source distributions. It also stops git and local directory dependencies (uv sync with uv 0.13.6 on 2026-10-30) and source archives, which also need a build; workspace members and editable requirements are still built. ↗
  • extra.durationStrings: Either a "friendly" duration (24 hours, 1 week, 30 days) or an ISO 8601 duration (PT24H, P7D, P30D). ↗
  • extra.durationUnits: Units in the documented examples: 24 hours, 1 week, 30 days. ↗
  • extra.excludeWildcards: exclude-newer-package keys are exact package names. ↗
  • extra.excludeValue: exclude-newer-package = { name = false } exempts the package from the delay. ↗
  • extra.missingUploadTime: Unless the package is exempt, or the index has its own exclude-newer value or is exempt with [[tool.uv.index]] exclude-newer = false. ↗
  • extra.requiredVersion: A PEP 440 specifier; uv exits with an error when its version does not match. Also at the top level of uv.toml. ↗
  • extra.lockedSettings: uv.lock records these in [options] (a relative exclude-newer as exclude-newer-span); after a change uv lock --check and uv sync --locked fail until uv lock runs again ("Resolving despite existing lockfile due to addition of global exclude newer", seen with uv 0.13.6 on 2026-10-30). no-build and no-binary-package are not recorded. ↗
  • extra.lockOptions: write_options writes [options] when exclude-newer is set: an absolute value as exclude-newer = "<timestamp>"; a relative one as exclude-newer = the placeholder "0001-01-01T00:00:00Z" (with the comment "This has no effect and is included for backwards compatibility when using relative exclude-newer values") and exclude-newer-span = the span as jiff prints it, ISO 8601 (P3D, P1W); since 0.11.8 exclude-newer may be missing next to exclude-newer-span. [options.exclude-newer-package] holds name = "<timestamp>", name = { timestamp = <placeholder>, span = "P3D" } or name = false. The placeholder is ExcludeNewerValue::PLACEHOLDER in crates/uv-distribution-types/src/exclude_newer.rs. ↗
  • extra.lockComparison: ExcludeNewer::compare (lock against the settings, in crates/uv-lock-operations/src/validated_lock.rs after lock.filter_exclude_newer) resolves again on an added or removed global value, a span that differs (ExcludeNewerSpan compares self.0.fieldwise() == other.0.fieldwise() in crates/uv-distribution-types/src/exclude_newer.rs, so "7 days" and P1W differ), a span added to or removed from an absolute value, and an absolute timestamp that is earlier than the locked one (a later one does not count); only a new timestamp of the same span is ignored. Per package (only names of locked packages and names the lock already records, filter_exclude_newer in crates/uv-lock/src/lock/mod.rs): a cutoff removed, a disabled one enabled, an enabled one added, or a changed value; an enabled one that is now disabled does not count. A date without a time (2026-01-01) is the start of the next day in the system time zone, so the timestamp it gives depends on the machine. Messages such as "Resolving despite existing lockfile due to change of exclude newer span from P3D to P1W" come from the Display of ExcludeNewerValueChange. ↗
  • extra.spanSyntax: jiff 0.2.37 is the version in Cargo.lock of uv 0.13.6. uv parses a relative exclude-newer with jiff's Span (FromStr of ExcludeNewerValue tries a timestamp, then a date, then a span), which reads the friendly format ("3 days", "1w", "2 weeks, 3 days", with each unit at most once, larger units first, a leading sign or a trailing "ago") and ISO 8601 (P3D, PT36H). Span prints as ISO 8601 by default, which is what uv.lock records. uv rejects years and months ("use days instead", crates/uv-distribution-types/src/exclude_newer.rs). The friendly format also has HH:MM:SS and fractions of hours and smaller units, which depcurfew does not read. ↗
  • extra.commandLine: clap arguments of uv add, uv pip install, uv tool install, uv run and uv tool run (uvx), with the global options, IndexArgs, ResolverInstallerArgs, BuildOptionsArgs and DependencyConstraintsArgs they share. --opt value, --opt=value, -o value and -ovalue all work; long options cannot be shortened. Options of uv run, uv tool run and uvx come before the command. --with, --with-editable and --with-executables-from add packages, as a list split at commas outside [...] that are not followed by !, =, <, > or ~ (comma.rs). uvx and uv tool run take the package from the first argument (name@version is ==version, name@latest the newest) unless --from names it; a first argument such as python, python3.12, pypy@3.8 or graalpy runs an interpreter (PythonRequest::try_from_tool_name in uv-python-types, implementation names cpython, pypy, graalpy and pyodide). uv run runs a command and downloads only what --with names, or a script given as an http or https URL. -e takes a path in uv pip install but is a flag in uv tool install, and --editable a flag in uv add; --no-binary and --only-binary take values in uv pip install only, -m is --marker in uv add and --module in uv run. ↗
  • extra.registryOverrides: IndexArgs (lib.rs): --index adds an index (name=url allowed), --default-index replaces PyPI, --index-url and --extra-index-url are their deprecated forms, --find-links adds a page or directory of archives; each has the variable named here. --config-file and UV_CONFIG_FILE name a uv.toml that may set any index. uv does not read pip.conf or PIP_INDEX_URL (docs/pip/compatibility.md). ↗
  • extra.selection: VersionSelectionArgs: --prerelease takes disallow, allow, if-necessary, explicit or if-necessary-or-explicit (UV_PRERELEASE); allow considers pre-releases of every package and disallow none (docs/concepts/resolution.md); --prerelease-package takes PACKAGE=MODE; the hidden --pre means allow. BuildOptionsArgs of uv add, uv run, uv tool install and uv tool run (uvx): --no-binary (UV_NO_BINARY, a boolean) installs every package from source, --binary turns it off, --no-binary-package names one package (UV_NO_BINARY_PACKAGE, separated by spaces). uv pip install takes --no-binary and --only-binary as lists separated by commas with :all: and :none:, as pip does (PipInstallArgs). ↗
  • extra.latestVersion: Released on 2026-10-26. ↗
  • extra.directoryOptions: GlobalArgs (global = true, so before or after the subcommand): --directory "Change to the given directory prior to running the command. Relative paths are resolved with the given directory as the base", env UV_WORKING_DIR; --project "Discover a project in the given directory. All pyproject.toml, uv.toml, and .python-version files will be discovered by walking up the directory tree from the project root … Other command-line arguments (such as relative paths) will be resolved relative to the current working directory … This setting has no effect when used in the uv pip interface", env UV_PROJECT. crates/uv-static/src/env_vars.rs: UV_WORKING_DIR is "Equivalent to the --directory command-line argument. UV_WORKING_DIRECTORY (added in v0.9.1) is also supported for backwards compatibility", UV_PROJECT "Equivalent to the --project command-line argument". ↗
  • extra.installOverrides: ExcludeNewerArgs: #[arg(long, env = EnvVars::UV_EXCLUDE_NEWER)] exclude_newer accepts RFC 3339 timestamps, local dates, friendly and ISO 8601 durations, and false to disable it; PackageExcludeNewerArgs adds --exclude-newer-package PACKAGE=DATE and is flattened into ResolverArgs, InstallerArgs and ResolverInstallerArgs. A date is not compared with the policy, so it is asked about. ↗
  • extra.pipTable: For uv pip commands, PipSettings::combine merges the [pip] table of a configuration file above its top-level keys ("Merge the top-level options (tool.uv) with the pip-specific options (tool.uv.pip), preferring the latter"): [pip] exclude-newer replaces the top-level value (false turns the delay off), [pip] exclude-newer-package entries win over top-level entries for the same package, and [pip] no-binary (package names, or :all:) is added to the top-level no-binary-package (NoBinary::combine in crates/uv-configuration/src/build_options.rs). [pip] no-build and only-binary are joined with the top-level no-build, so they cannot turn it off. Commands outside uv pip ignore [pip] ("These values will be ignored when running commands outside the uv pip namespace", PipOptions in crates/uv-settings/src/settings.rs). keys maps each top-level key depcurfew writes to the [pip] key that overrides or extends it. The same code in 0.11.32 (crates/uv/src/settings.rs). ↗

pip

  • cooldown.setting: Only files uploaded before the given time are considered; applies to packages from remote indexes, not to local files, find-links directories or VCS requirements. Isolated build environments get the same cutoff. ↗
  • cooldown.configFiles: pip reads no configuration from the project: only the global, user and site (virtual environment) pip.conf, the file named by PIP_CONFIG_FILE and PIP_* environment variables. --uploaded-prior-to is not allowed in requirements files, so a project sets it through PIP_UPLOADED_PRIOR_TO or the command line, e.g. in CI. ↗
  • cooldown.cliFlag: pip install, download, wheel, lock, index and list; environment variable PIP_UPLOADED_PRIOR_TO. ↗
  • cooldown.unit: A duration is written PnD, a whole number of days (P3D, matched by ^P(\d+)D$); P1W, PT24H and "7 days" are rejected. An ISO 8601 date or time is a fixed cutoff instead. A day is always 24 hours. ↗
  • cooldown.availableSince: pip 26.1 accepts a duration in days ("P3D for 3 days ago"); the option itself exists since 26.0 with dates only. ↗
  • cooldown.default: Off unless given. ↗
  • installScripts.defaultPolicy: pip builds a source distribution when no wheel fits, which runs its build backend; the secure-installs guide recommends --only-binary :all:. Installing a wheel runs no package code. ↗
  • installScripts.allowList.setting: After --only-binary=:all:, a package named in --no-binary may only be installed from source: a name wins over :all:, so its wheels are not used. --only-binary=:all: given after it clears the exception. ↗
  • installScripts.allowList.file: --only-binary and --no-binary are among the options a requirements file may contain. ↗
  • installScripts.allowList.format: Each use adds to the set; :all: and :none: are special values. ↗
  • installScripts.disableAll: Allowed in requirements files and pip.conf (only-binary). Enforced only for packages from indexes and find-links: VCS, local directory and direct URL source requirements are still built. ↗
  • project.lockfiles: pip lock (experimental since 25.1) writes pylock.toml; pip install -r pylock.toml is experimental since 26.1. The specification also allows pylock.<name>.toml (extra.lockfilePattern). ↗
  • project.manifests: "requirements.txt is usually what these files are named (although, that is not a requirement)"; the format is meant only for pip. ↗
  • user.files: The user file is pip.conf (pip.ini on Windows) in appdirs.user_config_dir("pip") (configuration.py), written in INI with [global] and per-command sections. Linux: $HOME/.config/pip/pip.conf, "which respects the XDG_CONFIG_HOME environment variable" (used when it is not empty after stripping spaces, vendored platformdirs _xdg.py). A relative XDG_CONFIG_HOME is used as it is, so pip reads <value>/pip/pip.conf relative to the directory each command runs in: XDGMixin.user_config_dir in src/pip/_vendor/platformdirs/_xdg.py at 26.2.2 returns the stripped value with no absolute-path check, _append_app_name_and_version (api.py) only joins the app name, and utils/appdirs.py and configuration.py (os.path.join, then os.path.exists and parser.read) make nothing absolute; 26.1.1 (platformdirs 4.5.1, Unix.user_config_dir in unix.py) does the same without stripping the value. macOS: ~/Library/Application Support/pip when that directory exists, else ~/.config/pip, where XDG_CONFIG_HOME is not used (utils/appdirs.py: _macos_user_config_dir takes platformdirs user_data_dir when os.path.isdir); since 26.2 that user_data_dir is $XDG_DATA_HOME/pip when XDG_DATA_HOME is not empty after stripping spaces, so with XDG_DATA_HOME set the Library directory is skipped and ~/.config/pip is used unless $XDG_DATA_HOME/pip exists (XDGMixin in src/pip/_vendor/platformdirs/_xdg.py at 26.2.2; src/pip/_vendor/vendor.txt names platformdirs 4.10.0 in 26.2 and 4.5.1 in 26.1.1, whose macos.py has no XDG handling). Windows: %APPDATA%\pip\pip.ini; platformdirs asks Windows for the Roaming AppData known folder, which APPDATA normally names. The legacy ~/.pip/pip.conf (%HOME%\pip\pip.ini) is loaded as well, below this file. Same path code in 25.0; earlier versions are not tracked. A command reads [global] and the section named after itself, which wins key by key (override_order ["global", self.name, ":env:"] in cli/parser.py); the options doctor --user checks (index_group and package_selection_group in cli/cmdoptions.py) belong to install, download, wheel, lock, index and list (src/pip/_internal/commands at 26.2.2). Keys are read without regard to case and with underscores as dashes, so only_binary in a file is the same setting as only-binary, and the later one wins (_normalize_name in src/pip/_internal/configuration.py at 26.2.2: name.lower().replace("_", "-"), applied to every key of every file). ↗
  • user.settings: depcurfew writes them in [global], which reaches every command that has the option (install, download, wheel, lock, index, list) and the resolver of pipenv lock; [install] reaches only pip install. "Accept --no-binary and --only-binary via pip.conf" (7.1.0); uploaded-prior-to is read from pip.conf like every long option ("To override a duration set in configuration, pass P0D on the command line", user_guide.rst). 26.1.0 because depcurfew writes a PnD duration: pip 26.0 takes dates only and aborts on P7D, and 25.x ignores the key. uv does not read pip.conf. ↗
  • user.unknownKeys: "Ignore options not present in this parser": a key that is not an option of the command is skipped without a word (same code in 25.0), so pip before 26.0 ignores uploaded-prior-to. ↗
  • user.invalidValueFails: A pip.conf that is not valid INI stops pip (ConfigurationFileCouldNotBeLoaded, configuration.py), and a value an option rejects ends the command with a usage error: pip 26.0 aborts on uploaded-prior-to = P7D in every command that has the option, as it rejected PIP_UPLOADED_PRIOR_TO=P100000D in a test on 2026-10-30 (configuration values and PIP_* variables go through the same parsing). Commands without the option ignore the key. ↗
  • user.weakening: In any section of any pip.conf: [install] beats [global], and a later file (a virtual environment pip.conf, PIP_CONFIG_FILE) beats the user file per key, an empty value removing it (cli/parser.py). :all: in one of the lists clears the other and :none: clears its own (handle_mutual_excludes). P0D overrides a configured duration, and "Local package files are allowed regardless of the --uploaded-prior-to setting", find-links directories included (user_guide.rst). A requirements file can also pass --no-binary or --only-binary, but not --uploaded-prior-to. ↗
  • user.variables: "Note that if a file exists at the location that this is set to, the user config file will not be loaded." PIP_<NAME> variables are applied after the [global] and command sections of all files, and the command line beats them (cli/parser.py, override_order). --isolated, a flag, ignores environment variables and user configuration. XDG_CONFIG_HOME (Linux) and XDG_DATA_HOME (macOS, 26.2) move the user directory (see files). ↗
  • extra.configCommand: pip config: "Manage local and global configuration"; "set: Set the command.option=value", "unset: Unset the value associated with command.option", "edit: Edit the configuration file in an editor". Without --user, --global or --site it changes the configuration file of the active virtual environment when that file exists, otherwise the user file. So a CI step or RUN with pip config set can give the later pip installs a release-age delay that no environment variable shows (decision D-115). ↗
  • extra.configSections: "Each subcommand can be configured optionally in its own section. This overrides the global setting with the same name", and "A command specific section in the config file [<command>] host = bar overrides the option with same name in the [global] config file section" (Precedence / Override order). pip reads only [global], the section of the running command and :env: (override_order = ["global", self.name, ":env:"] in cli/parser.py), so a managed key in [install] wins over [global] for pip install; depcurfew comments such keys out in every section other than [global]. ↗
  • extra.pylockCreatedBy: pip lock writes created-by = "pip". ↗
  • extra.requirementsOptions: SUPPORTED_OPTIONS in pip 26.2.2 (--pypi-url is a second name of --index-url in cmdoptions.py); per requirement also --hash and -C/--config-settings (extra.requirementsParser). Any other option, --uploaded-prior-to included, makes the file fail to parse. ↗
  • extra.requirementsParser: build_parser reads each line with optparse.OptionParser(add_help_option=False) holding SUPPORTED_OPTIONS and SUPPORTED_OPTIONS_REQ (--hash, -C/--config-settings), with the names from cmdoptions.py in pip 26.2.2. optparse takes a long option shortened to a prefix that names exactly one of them (_match_abbrev; an exact name wins), so --no-bin :all: is --no-binary :all:, and an ambiguous prefix such as --no is an error. --hash and --config-settings belong to the requirement on their line; on a line of their own they are accepted and ignored. ↗
  • extra.configFileNames: CONFIG_BASENAME is pip.ini on Windows and pip.conf elsewhere; pip looks for it only in the global, user and site locations, never in the project. ↗
  • extra.configFileVariable: Names a configuration file pip loads last; set to os.devnull it turns all configuration files off. ↗
  • extra.environmentVariable: Every long option has a PIP_<UPPER_LONG_NAME> variable with dashes replaced by underscores. Tested on 2026-10-30: pip 25.3 ignored PIP_UPLOADED_PRIOR_TO=P100000D without a word, 26.0 rejected the duration (dates only), 26.1 and 26.2.2 found no version. ↗
  • extra.durationStrings: Only whole days, written PnD, are accepted. ↗
  • extra.missingUploadTime: Upload times exist only in the JSON form of the simple repository API (PEP 700); indexes that serve only HTML have none. ↗
  • extra.lockfileFormat: Each [[packages]] entry has exactly one of vcs (always built), directory (always built), archive (a source tree or wheel at a URL or path) or sdist and wheels from an index. A package with an sdist and no wheels is built when installed. A relative directory path is relative to the lock file. The required top-level created-by records the name of the tool that wrote the file, preferably its normalized Python package name. ↗
  • extra.requirementsFormat: VCS requirements start with git+, hg+, svn+ or bzr+, alone or after "name @"; a direct reference is "name @ URL" (PEP 508); -e installs a project path or VCS URL in editable mode. The legacy #egg= fragment names the project of a VCS URL. Lines ending with a backslash continue on the next line, and # starts a comment (requirements-file-format.md). ↗
  • extra.requirementsIncludes: Option names from cli/cmdoptions.py. A relative path is relative to the directory of the file that includes it, and a file given as an http, https or file URL is downloaded (req_file.py, _parse_and_recurse); a file that includes itself is an error. ↗
  • extra.requirementsPaths: A requirement that contains a path separator (/, and \ on Windows) or starts with . looks like a path (_looks_like_path): a directory there is a project built from source. A name with an archive extension (utils/filetypes.py) is a local archive, even when the file does not exist. http, https, ftp and file URLs are links (utils/urls.py, is_url). ↗
  • extra.lockfilePattern: A named lock file, next to or instead of pylock.toml; pip lock -o writes any name. ↗
  • extra.commandLine: pip and pip3 are the console scripts of the package (pyproject.toml). The options of pip install (install.py) and the general options of every command (cmdoptions.py, general_group), which may also come before install. pip reads them with optparse: --opt value and --opt=value, -rFILE and -r FILE, grouped short flags, and a long option shortened to any prefix that names one option (cli/parser.py keeps optparse's matching), so --extra means --extra-index-url. -e takes a local project path or a VCS URL to install in editable mode. -r, -c, --requirements-from-script and --group read requirements from files of the project. ↗
  • extra.registryOverrides: index_group: --index-url (alias -i, --pypi-url) is the base URL of the package index, --extra-index-url adds indexes, --find-links (-f) adds an HTML page or a directory of archives. Every long option can be set as PIP_<UPPER_LONG_NAME>, dashes replaced by underscores (docs/html/topics/configuration.md); PIP_CONFIG_FILE names a configuration file loaded last, which may set any index. Requirements files may set --index-url, --extra-index-url and --find-links too (req/req_file.py). ↗
  • extra.selection: --pre includes pre-release and development versions (pip only finds stable versions by default) and stands for --all-releases :all:; --all-releases and --only-final take :all:, :none: or package names separated by commas and allow or forbid pre-releases of those packages (models/release_control.py, allows_prereleases: a named package first, then :all:). --no-binary and --only-binary take the same lists: --no-binary keeps pip from using wheels, so the package is built from source, --only-binary from using source distributions (models/format_control.py, handle_mutual_excludes and get_allowed_formats). Each long option can also be set as PIP_<NAME> (docs/html/topics/configuration.md), which the command line overrides. ↗
  • extra.pipxSelection: pipx forwards the standard PIP_* variables to pip (except PIP_USER, PIP_DISABLE_PIP_VERSION_CHECK, PIP_TARGET and PIP_KEYRING_PROVIDER); the uv backend ignores them (docs/how-to/use-private-index.rst). pipx has no option of its own for pre-releases or source builds; --pip-args may carry them. ↗
  • extra.pipxCommandLine: pipx installs applications from PyPI with pip (or uv) in environments of their own. pipx install takes one or more package specs, a VCS URL, a local path or archive, or a script (--preinstall adds packages to the environment; -f is --force, not find-links). pipx run takes the app first (argparse REMAINDER, so its options come before it), --spec names the package to install and run, --with adds packages; a first argument ending in .py is a local script, or a script to download when it is a URL (docs/how-to/run-scripts.rst). argparse accepts abbreviated long options. ↗
  • extra.pipxRegistryOverrides: --index-url is the base URL of the index; --pip-args is split and handed to pip, so it may carry --extra-index-url, --find-links or --no-index. With the pip backend pipx passes the PIP_* variables on to pip; with the uv backend it ignores them and uv reads its own (docs/how-to/use-uv-backend.rst); both kinds are registry variables of pip and uv. ↗
  • extra.latestVersion: Released on 2026-10-20. ↗
  • extra.configCommands: pip config list, edit, get, set, unset and debug: set and unset change a value and save the file, edit opens it in an editor, list, get and debug only read. Without --user, --global or --site the site file is used if one exists, else the user file. ↗
  • extra.installOverrides: --uploaded-prior-to (metavar datetime_or_duration): "Only consider packages uploaded prior to the given value", an ISO 8601 datetime or a duration of whole days P<n>D. PIP_UPLOADED_PRIOR_TO is its environment variable (environmentVariable). A datetime is not compared with the policy, so it is asked about. ↗

Poetry

  • cooldown.setting: A version is ignored while any of its files was uploaded less than the given number of days ago. Applies when dependencies are resolved (poetry lock, add, update); poetry install installs the locked versions without checking. ↗
  • cooldown.configFiles: The local configuration next to pyproject.toml (poetry config --local), not pyproject.toml itself. Environment variables (POETRY_SOLVER_MIN_RELEASE_AGE) win over poetry.toml, which wins over the user config.toml. ↗
  • cooldown.unit: An integer; 0 (the default) turns it off. ↗
  • cooldown.availableSince: "Introduced in 2.4.0"; announced as dependency cooldowns. Tested on 2026-10-30: Poetry 2.3.4 locked with min-release-age = 100000 in poetry.toml without a word, while 2.4.0 and 2.5.1 found no version. ↗
  • cooldown.default: Default 0: all versions are considered. ↗
  • installScripts.defaultPolicy: Without installer.only-binary, packages are built from source distributions when no wheel fits, which runs their build backend. Git and directory dependencies are always built. Installing a wheel runs no package code. ↗
  • installScripts.allowList.setting: With installer.only-binary = [":all:"], the packages named in installer.no-binary are the only ones built from source; explicit names take precedence over :all: since 2.2.0. Their wheels are rejected, so they are always built. ↗
  • installScripts.allowList.file: The docs recommend configuring it with --local for a project. ↗
  • installScripts.allowList.availableSince: "explicit package names will take precedence over :all:" (#10278). Before 2.2.0 a package listed in both settings has no file left to install. ↗
  • installScripts.allowList.format: poetry config stores the value as a list; written by hand, a boolean is not normalized. ↗
  • installScripts.disableAll: Since 2.0.0; the value [":all:"] forbids source distributions for every package. Applied only when installing: resolving dependencies may still build source distributions without static metadata, and git, path and url dependencies are built regardless. ↗
  • project.lockfiles: Next to pyproject.toml. ↗
  • user.files: "Linux: $XDG_CONFIG_HOME/pypoetry or ~/.config/pypoetry - Windows: %APPDATA%\pypoetry - macOS: ~/Library/Application Support/pypoetry". The code agrees on Linux and Windows: CONFIG_DIR = POETRY_CONFIG_DIR or user_config_path("pypoetry", appauthor=False, roaming=True) from platformdirs, without an override (src/poetry/locations.py; on Windows platformdirs asks for the Roaming AppData known folder, which APPDATA normally names). On macOS the directory depends on the installed platformdirs (libraryDirectories); the docs' directory is used when its version cannot be read. config.toml holds the settings as nested tables ([solver] min-release-age = 7); auth.toml next to it holds credentials. POETRY_CONFIG_DIR replaces the directory. Checked at 2.4.0 and 2.5.1; 2.0.0 removed the special handling of the platformdirs 2 macOS directory (#8916, CHANGELOG.md), and older versions are not tracked. ↗
  • user.libraryDirectories: Poetry 2.5.1 allows platformdirs >=3.0.0,<5 (pyproject.toml; its own lock has 4.11.8), and the official installer runs pip install without constraints in Poetry's virtual environment (python-poetry/install.python-poetry.org, install-poetry.py), so a new install gets the latest 4.x. The macOS user_config_dir by platformdirs version, read from the code at the tags: before 4.6.0 always ~/Library/Application Support/<app> (4.5.1 macos.py has no XDG support); 4.6.0 "Honor XDG environment variables on macOS" (#375): a non-empty XDG_CONFIG_HOME wins, a relative one too (os.environ.get("XDG_CONFIG_HOME", "").strip() in _xdg.py), and otherwise the config directory is user_data_dir, so a non-empty XDG_DATA_HOME gives $XDG_DATA_HOME/<app>; 4.9.6 "Fix macOS XDG variables leaking across user_config_dir, user_data_dir, and user_state_dir" (#473; 4.9.4 still returns user_data_dir); 4.11.8 "Ignore relative paths in XDG Base Directory environment variables and use the existing platform fallback" (#540, _xdg_dir with posixpath.isabs); the same in 4.14.2. A relative value is used as it is, so the tool reads it from the directory each command runs in. ↗
  • user.settings: Every setting of Config.default_config is read from the user config.toml ("As with all configurations described here, this is a user specific configuration", docs/configuration.md); dotted names are nested tables. A project poetry.toml replaces a user value, lists included (merge_dicts in src/poetry/utils/helpers.py), and POETRY_* variables beat both. Names in installer.no-binary win over installer.only-binary = :all: only since 2.2.0. solver.min-release-age-exclude is written as a TOML array (see extra.excludeFormat). ↗
  • user.weakening: Precedence: environment variables, then the local poetry.toml, then the global config.toml, then the default; a poetry.toml key replaces the user value, lists included. solver.min-release-age-exclude-source (2.4.0) takes source names from pyproject.toml or URLs. A source that gives no upload times is not filtered at all. ↗
  • user.variables: Any setting can be set as POETRY_ plus its name in capitals with dots and dashes replaced by underscores, and environment variables come first in the precedence ("Configuration sources"). POETRY_CONFIG_DIR replaces the platform directory (src/poetry/locations.py). ↗
  • extra.installedPackage: The installation page installs a given version with "pipx install poetry==1.8.4", in CI with "$POETRY_HOME/bin/pip install poetry==2.0.0", and manually with pip in a virtual environment ("$VENV_PATH/bin/pip install poetry"). So poetry==<version> in such a command of a CI job or a Dockerfile is the Poetry the later commands run (decision D-115). ↗
  • extra.pylockCreatedBy: Poetry exports pylock.toml with its export plugin (poetry export --format pylock.toml), which writes created-by = "poetry-plugin-export". ↗
  • extra.configTable: poetry config writes dotted settings as nested tables, e.g. [solver] min-release-age = 7. ↗
  • extra.pyprojectTable: A [tool.poetry] table marks a Poetry project; Poetry also accepts a [project] table with a static name and version, which other tools use as well. ↗
  • extra.buildSwitch: installer.only-binary was added in 2.0.0; [":all:"] forbids source distributions of index packages when installing. Git and directory dependencies, and source archives from a URL or path, are built regardless (src/poetry/installation/executor.py at 2.5.1). ↗
  • extra.excludeWildcards: Package names only. ↗
  • extra.requiredVersion: A version constraint in pyproject.toml; a Poetry version outside it raises an error. Poetry 1.x does not know the key. ↗
  • extra.commandLine: poetry add takes package specs: a name, name@constraint, a PEP 508 requirement, a git URL, a path or an archive URL. Options with a value: --group (-G), --extras (-E), --optional, --python, --platform, --markers and --source of add, and the global --directory (-C) and --project (-P); -e/--editable, -D/--dev, --allow-prereleases, --dry-run and --lock are flags (src/poetry/console/commands/add.py). cleo reads -Gdocs and -G docs; long options cannot be shortened. poetry install takes no package specs. ↗
  • extra.registryOverrides: --source names a package source of [[tool.poetry.source]] in pyproject.toml (or pypi) to take the package from. The POETRY_REPOSITORIES_<NAME>_URL variables are for publishing only; no variable sets a package source. ↗
  • extra.versionConstraints: poetry add name@constraint takes any constraint valid in pyproject.toml, and name@latest the newest release; extras go before the @ (name[extra]@^4.0.0). ^ keeps the left-most non-zero part of major.minor.patch: ^1.2.3 is >=1.2.3 <2.0.0, ^1.2 is >=1.2.0 <2.0.0, ^1 is >=1.0.0 <2.0.0, ^0.2.3 is >=0.2.3 <0.3.0, ^0.0.3 is >=0.0.3 <0.0.4, ^0.0 is >=0.0.0 <0.1.0, ^0 is >=0.0.0 <1.0.0. ~ allows patch changes, or minor changes when only a major version is given: ~1.2.3 is >=1.2.3 <1.3.0, ~1.2 is >=1.2.0 <1.3.0, ~1 is >=1.0.0 <2.0.0. * is any version, 1.* is >=1.0.0 <2.0.0, 1.2.* is >=1.2.0 <1.3.0. ~=, inequalities and == as in PEP 440, several separated by commas; a bare version (1.2.3) is that version only. Poetry prefers stable releases and takes a pre-release only when no stable release satisfies the constraint. ↗
  • extra.selection: installer.no-binary (POETRY_INSTALLER_NO_BINARY) disallows wheels for :all: or true, none for :none: or false, or the named packages separated by commas; installer.only-binary (POETRY_INSTALLER_ONLY_BINARY, since 2.0.0) takes the same values; explicit package names win over :all:. poetry add --allow-prereleases accepts pre-releases (src/poetry/console/commands/add.py). ↗
  • extra.latestVersion: Released on 2026-09-20. ↗
  • extra.directoryOptions: Global options: --directory=DIRECTORY (-C) "The working directory for the Poetry command (defaults to the current working directory). All command-line arguments will be resolved relative to the given directory."; --project=PROJECT (-P) "Specify another path as the project root. All command-line arguments will be resolved relative to the current working directory or directory specified using --directory option if used." The page names no environment variable for either. ↗
  • extra.registrySettings: [[tool.poetry.source]] has name, url and priority. "If priority is undefined, the source is considered a primary source"; "All primary package sources are searched for each dependency without a source constraint" and "If you configure at least one primary source, the implicit PyPI source is disabled", so a primary source replaces PyPI. Supplemental sources "are only searched if no other (higher-priority) source yields a compatible package distribution": depcurfew does not know whether PyPI has a version the constraint takes, so such a source counts as one that adds. Explicit sources "are only searched when a package configuration explicitly indicates" them: a dependency with source = "name" in [tool.poetry.dependencies] or [tool.poetry.group.<group>.dependencies], also in a list of constraint tables (docs/dependency-specification.md, docs/managing-dependencies.md). create_pool in src/poetry/factory.py reads Priority[source.get("priority", "primary").upper()], so the value is compared without regard to case; the schema (src/poetry/json/schemas/poetry.json) allows primary, supplemental and explicit, and another value (default or secondary of Poetry 1.x) fails in 2.5.1 and counts as primary here. A source whose name is pypi in any case is PyPI itself (PyPiRepository), which "cannot be configured with a custom url" (create_package_source). ↗
  • extra.configCommands: poetry config [options] [setting-key] [setting-value1] ... [setting-valueN] sets a key to a value; --unset removes the key, --migrate migrates outdated settings and --list only shows them. ↗
  • extra.installOverrides: Settings can be set with environment variables prefixed by POETRY_, the upper-case setting name with dots and dashes replaced by underscores; solver.min-release-age (int, days) is POETRY_SOLVER_MIN_RELEASE_AGE. poetry add has no option for it. ↗

PDM

  • cooldown.setting: In the [tool.pdm.resolution] table of pyproject.toml: distributions uploaded after the cutoff are left out when dependencies are resolved (pdm lock, add, update). The --exclude-newer option of pdm lock wins over it, and it wins over strategy.exclude-newer in the PDM configuration. ↗
  • cooldown.configFiles: The [tool.pdm.resolution] table. ↗
  • cooldown.cliFlag: pdm lock --exclude-newer, since 2.13.0. ↗
  • cooldown.unit: An RFC 3339 timestamp, a UTC date or a relative duration N{d|h|w} such as 7d, 12h or 3w, counted from the current UTC time. ↗
  • cooldown.availableSince: "Support exclude-newer in pyproject.toml in the [tool.pdm.resolution] table" (#3776); the pdm lock option exists since 2.13.0. ↗
  • cooldown.default: No default: without a value every upload time is accepted. ↗
  • cooldown.exclude.setting: A table [tool.pdm.resolution.exclude-newer-override] of package = false (no cutoff) or package = another date or duration; package-specific values win over the global cutoff. ↗
  • cooldown.exclude.availableSince: "Allow package-specific overrides for exclude-newer resolution limits" (#3809). ↗
  • installScripts.defaultPolicy: Without only-binary, PDM locks and installs source distributions when they fit, which builds them and runs their build backend. Installing a wheel runs no package code. ↗
  • installScripts.allowList.setting: The packages in no-binary are locked and installed from source distributions only. A name in no-binary wins over only-binary = ":all:" (FormatControl of unearth, which PDM uses), so with both the named packages are the only ones built, and always from source. ↗
  • installScripts.allowList.file: The [tool.pdm.resolution] table. ↗
  • installScripts.allowList.availableSince: no-binary, only-binary and prefer-binary keys in [tool.pdm.resolution] (#2656). ↗
  • installScripts.disableAll: only-binary = ":all:" locks and installs only wheels. The variables PDM_ONLY_BINARY and PDM_NO_BINARY win over pyproject.toml. ↗
  • project.lockfiles: pdm.lock next to pyproject.toml; pdm lock --lockfile writes another name. ↗
  • user.files: <CONFIG_ROOT> is "$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/pdm (~/.config/pdm in most cases) on Linux", "~/Library/Application Support/pdm on macOS" and "%USERPROFILE%\AppData\Local\pdm\pdm on Windows". The code agrees on Linux and Windows: global_config = platformdirs.user_config_path("pdm") / "config.toml", without an override (src/pdm/project/core.py; on Windows the default appauthor repeats the name and roaming is off, so platformdirs asks for the Local AppData known folder). On macOS the directory depends on the installed platformdirs (libraryDirectories); the docs' directory is used when its version cannot be read. The home configuration is TOML whose nested tables are read as dotted keys ([strategy] exclude-newer is strategy.exclude-newer, load_config in src/pdm/project/config.py). -c/--config and PDM_CONFIG_FILE replace it; the site configuration is merged below it. Checked at 2.29.5; earlier versions are not tracked. ↗
  • user.libraryDirectories: PDM 2.29.5 does not pin platformdirs (pyproject.toml; its own lock has 4.9.6). The macOS user_config_dir by platformdirs version, read from the code at the tags: before 4.6.0 always ~/Library/Application Support/<app> (4.5.1 macos.py has no XDG support); 4.6.0 "Honor XDG environment variables on macOS" (#375): a non-empty XDG_CONFIG_HOME wins, a relative one too (os.environ.get("XDG_CONFIG_HOME", "").strip() in _xdg.py), and otherwise the config directory is user_data_dir, so a non-empty XDG_DATA_HOME gives $XDG_DATA_HOME/<app>; 4.9.6 "Fix macOS XDG variables leaking across user_config_dir, user_data_dir, and user_state_dir" (#473; 4.9.4 still returns user_data_dir); 4.11.8 "Ignore relative paths in XDG Base Directory environment variables and use the existing platform fallback" (#540, _xdg_dir with posixpath.isabs); the same in 4.14.2. A relative value is used as it is, so the tool reads it from the directory each command runs in. ↗
  • user.settings: "Allow configuring the default lock --exclude-newer value with strategy.exclude-newer" (2.27.0, #3795): a TOML string such as "7d", a date or an RFC 3339 time. It applies where neither [tool.pdm.resolution] exclude-newer in pyproject.toml nor a project pdm.toml sets it (docs/usage/lockfile.md), and with it in effect pdm lock resolves every package again (src/pdm/cli/commands/lock.py). only-binary, no-binary and exclude-newer-override cannot be set in any PDM configuration file: PDM reads them from [tool.pdm.resolution] in pyproject.toml, only-binary and no-binary also from PDM_ONLY_BINARY and PDM_NO_BINARY (environments/base.py, Project.env_or_setting), so at user level they can be set only through those variables. ↗
  • user.weakening: use_uv (experimental since 2.19.0, PDM_USE_UV) can be set in config.toml and in a project pdm.toml, which wins. PDM passes the cutoff and the build settings to uv lock but not to uv sync --frozen (src/pdm/installers/uv.py), and passes no --no-config, so uv also reads the user uv.toml. A home strategy.exclude-newer cannot weaken a project that sets [tool.pdm.resolution] exclude-newer. use_uv is a switch that turns a protection off and is read with ensure_boolean (src/pdm/project/config.py at 2.29.5): a TOML boolean or number is kept, and a string is false only when empty or false, no or 0 in any case; depcurfew counts every other value as on (other spellings of false too, which errs towards a warning). ↗
  • user.variables: env_or_setting returns os.getenv(var) or the pyproject.toml setting, so a non-empty PDM_ONLY_BINARY or PDM_NO_BINARY wins (environments/base.py). -c/--config and PDM_CONFIG_FILE name the home config file (src/pdm/core.py). PDM_USE_UV is the variable of use_uv, and variables beat pdm.toml and config.toml (src/pdm/project/config.py). ↗
  • extra.pylockCreatedBy: PDM's pylock.toml export writes created-by = "pdm". ↗
  • extra.configTables: exclude-newer, its overrides, only-binary and no-binary share the table. ↗
  • extra.pyprojectTable: PDM keeps its settings in [tool.pdm] of pyproject.toml. ↗
  • extra.buildSwitch: only-binary in [tool.pdm.resolution] since 2.15.0 (#2656). It covers packages from an index: git, URL and path requirements already name their source and are built regardless (candidates.py). ↗
  • extra.durationStrings: "pdm lock --exclude-newer now accepts relative durations in the format N{d|h|w}" (#3760). ↗
  • extra.durationUnits: Written as Nh, Nd and Nw. ↗
  • extra.excludeWildcards: Override keys are package names. ↗
  • extra.excludeValue: package = false disables the cutoff for that package. ↗
  • extra.missingUploadTime: The index must give upload-time (PEP 700); an override of false lifts it for the package. ↗
  • extra.lockedSettings: pdm.lock keeps a content_hash of the whole [tool.pdm.resolution] table (PyProject.content_hash), or with canonical lock inputs (lock_inputs = true, since 2.28.1) the table itself (src/pdm/project/lockfile/freshness.py), so a change to any key in it, exclude-newer and only-binary included, makes the lockfile out of date. pdm lock --check and pdm install --check then exit with 1 ("Lockfile doesn't satisfy the project requirements"), pdm install without --check locks again first and pdm sync only warns (check_lockfile in src/pdm/cli/actions.py) until pdm lock runs again; with exclude-newer set, pdm lock resolves every package again (src/pdm/cli/commands/lock.py). ↗
  • extra.lockHash: PyProject.content_hash: json.dumps(dump_data, sort_keys=True) of sources (tool.pdm.source, default []), dependencies, dev-dependencies, optional-dependencies (default {}), requires-python (default "") and resolution (tool.pdm.resolution, default {}), encoded as UTF-8 and hashed with hashlib (sha256). dev-dependencies are the [dependency-groups] tables, each name normalized (normalize_name in src/pdm/utils.py: re.sub(r"[^A-Za-z0-9]+", "-", name).lower(); a name twice is an error), then the lists of tool.pdm.dev-dependencies appended under their normalized names. pdm.lock keeps it as [metadata] content_hash = "sha256:<hex>" (src/pdm/project/lockfile/pdmlock.py). Project.is_lockfile_fresh (src/pdm/project/core.py) compares it only while pdm.lock has no metadata.lock_inputs and resolution.lock_inputs is not true; a workspace root (tool.pdm.workspace set, WorkspaceManager.is_root in src/pdm/project/workspace.py) hashes the members too. PDM reads pyproject.toml with tomllib (src/pdm/project/toml_file.py), and without a [project] table it converts a Poetry or Flit project first (_convert_pyproject). ↗
  • extra.directoryOptions: project_option: "-p", "--project", dest="project_path", help "Specify another path as the project root, which changes the base of pyproject.toml and __pypackages__ [env var: PDM_PROJECT]", default=os.getenv("PDM_PROJECT"). ↗
  • extra.commandLine: pdm add takes requirements as arguments and editable ones with -e; its options come from cli/options.py and the global ones (-c/--config, --pep582, -I, -n, --no-cache) from core.py. --venv and --pep582 take an optional value, so argparse takes the next word for it. PDM parses with argparse, which accepts abbreviated long options. ↗
  • extra.registrySettings: [[tool.pdm.source]] has name, url, verify_ssl, type ("index or find_links, default to index") and include_packages and exclude_packages. "If you want to omit the default PyPI index, just set the source name to pypi and that source will replace it"; the source list is pypi.url (when no source is named pypi), then the sources of pyproject.toml, then pypi.<name>.url of the configuration, and by default "all sources are treated as equal" and the best matching version across them is taken, so any other source adds an index. Project.get_sources (src/pdm/project/core.py) keys the sources by name and fills a source named pypi from pypi.url, so one without a URL is PyPI (or the pypi.url of the user's configuration, which depcurfew does not read); PDM compares the name pypi exactly, depcurfew without regard to case, which only compares more URLs. Variables in url (${PROJECT_ROOT}) are expanded by PDM, so depcurfew cannot read such a URL. include_packages and exclude_packages are not read: a source counts for every package. ↗
  • extra.registryOverrides: pypi.url, the index PDM uses when pyproject.toml names no source called pypi, can be set with PDM_PYPI_URL (docs/usage/config.md); -c/--config and PDM_CONFIG_FILE name another configuration file, which may set any index. ↗
  • extra.selection: pdm add --pre/--prerelease allows pre-releases of the added packages to be pinned and --stable only stable versions (prerelease_option; commands/add.py sets req.prerelease). PDM_NO_BINARY and PDM_ONLY_BINARY are lists of packages separated by commas, :all: for every package, of which no wheel, or only wheels, are locked and installed (docs/usage/lockfile.md; environments/base.py, _setting_list). ↗
  • extra.latestVersion: Released on 2026-10-30. ↗

Pipenv

  • cooldown.setting: In the [pipenv] section of the Pipfile: the resolver considers only versions uploaded at least that many days ago. Pipenv computes a cutoff (now minus N days, UTC) and hands it to pip as uploaded-prior-to. ↗
  • cooldown.configFiles: The [pipenv] section. ↗
  • cooldown.unit: A string in <int>d format, e.g. "30d"; any other form is rejected (pipenv/vendor/plette/models/sections.py). ↗
  • cooldown.availableSince: "Added support for cool-down-period in the [pipenv] section of the Pipfile" under 2026.6.2 (repeated under 2026.7.0 and 2026.7.1). Checked against the wheels on 2026-10-30: missing in 2026.6.1, present in 2026.6.2. ↗
  • cooldown.default: Off unless set. ↗
  • installScripts.defaultPolicy: Pipenv installs with pip, which builds source distributions when no wheel fits. Pipenv honors pip configuration and PIP_* variables, which may forbid building (PIP_ONLY_BINARY=:all:), but the Pipfile has no setting for it. ↗
  • project.lockfiles: JSON, generated next to the Pipfile. ↗
  • project.manifests: TOML. ↗
  • user.files: Pipenv has no user configuration file of its own: it reads environment variables (set in a shell profile), the project .env file and pip configuration; pipenv/environments.py looks up no file. Since 2026.3.0 the pip install step runs with PIP_CONFIG_FILE=/dev/null (pipenv/utils/pip.py; CHANGELOG.md, #6066), so a user pip.conf no longer protects pipenv install and sync. The lock resolver still reads pip.conf, but only [global], because its pip command is named InstallCommand (pipenv/utils/resolver.py), and a Pipfile cool-down-period replaces its uploaded-prior-to. ↗
  • user.settings: No user file to write to; at user level only PIP_* variables reach the pip install step (see variables). ↗
  • user.unknownKeys: No user file. ↗
  • user.invalidValueFails: No user file. ↗
  • user.weakening: No user file; see variables. ↗
  • extra.pylockCreatedBy: Pipenv writes created-by = "pipenv" in the pylock.toml it generates (docs/pylock.md). pipenv install -e . writes the project itself as { path = ".", editable = true } (docs/pipfile.md), and Pipfile.lock keeps it as "path": ".". ↗
  • extra.configTables: Directives such as cool-down-period and allow_prereleases live in [pipenv]. ↗
  • extra.durationStrings: Only whole days (<int>d) are accepted. ↗
  • extra.durationUnits: Whole days only. ↗
  • extra.missingUploadTime: "When the index does not provide upload-time metadata (e.g. most private mirrors) the setting is accepted but has no filtering effect." ↗
  • extra.lockfileFormat: Every key but _meta is a category (default, develop and custom ones) mapping names to entries: index packages have version, hashes and index; git entries git and ref; remote archives file; local projects path, and editable = true for editable installs (Pipfile "Packages Section"). The hashes of wheels and source distributions are listed together, so the lockfile does not show which packages are built. ↗
  • extra.commandLine: pipenv install takes packages as arguments (without any it installs the Pipfile) and editable ones with -e; -r imports a requirements file. The options of install and the global flags of pipenv, read with argparse, which accepts abbreviated long options. ↗
  • extra.registryOverrides: --index names a package index by URL or by a source name of the Pipfile; --pypi-mirror and PIPENV_PYPI_MIRROR replace PyPI. --extra-pip-args hands arguments to pip, which may name another index, and Pipenv loads PIP_* variables from the .env file that PIPENV_DOTENV_LOCATION names (options.py). ↗
  • extra.directoryOptions: Pipfile.location returns project.s.PIPENV_PIPFILE when it is set, and only otherwise searches the working directory and its parents (find_pipfile, PIPENV_MAX_DEPTH); project_directory is str(Path(self.location).parent.absolute()), and Project.__init__ (pipenv/project.py) changes to it for every command but run. pipenv/environments.py: "If set, this specifies a custom Pipfile location. When running pipenv from a location other than the same directory where the Pipfile is located, instruct pipenv to find the Pipfile in the location specified by this environment variable"; the path is taken relative to the working directory and made absolute. pipenv/cli/options.py has no option that moves the project (--where only prints it). ↗
  • extra.registrySettings: Each [[source]] has name, url and verify_ssl. "all packages are mapped to a single package index for security reasons"; "To install a package from a specific index, you must match the name of the index" (torch = {version = "*", index = "pytorch"} in [packages]); to use another index as the default, "simply omit PyPI from your sources". A package without index comes from the default index, the first source: Resolver.create in pipenv/utils/resolver.py takes project.sources.get_default_index()["name"], which is pipfile_sources()[0] (pipenv/utils/sources.py), and without [[source]] the default is https://pypi.org/simple (pipenv/project.py). With install_search_all_sources = true in [pipenv], "pipenv install and pipenv sync will search all configured sources for packages, but only during installation", so every source adds. Names are compared exactly (get_index_by_name), depcurfew without regard to case; package categories are any top-level table ([packages], [dev-packages] and custom ones). Variables in url are expanded by Pipenv (expand_url_credentials), so depcurfew cannot read such a URL. ↗
  • extra.selection: pipenv install --pre allows pre-releases (and records allow_prereleases in the Pipfile, routines/install.py). Pipenv installs with pip, which reads PIP_NO_BINARY and PIP_ONLY_BINARY like --no-binary and --only-binary (see buildSwitch). ↗
  • extra.latestVersion: Released on 2026-10-28. ↗

Renovate

  • cooldown.setting: Formerly stabilityDays; old configs are migrated to "N days". Can be scoped with packageRules, vulnerabilityAlerts and update types. ↗
  • cooldown.configFiles: Searched in this order. The "renovate" key in package.json still works but is deprecated. Shared presets (extends) can also set it. ↗
  • cooldown.cliFlag: Self-hosted CLI only; environment variable RENOVATE_MINIMUM_RELEASE_AGE. ↗
  • cooldown.unit: For example "3 days" or "14 days". Since 42.19.5 "0 days" is treated like null. ↗
  • cooldown.availableSince: Earlier versions had the same feature as stabilityDays (first version not tracked). ↗
  • cooldown.default: Off unless configured; the docs table shows no default. config:best-practices adds "3 days" for npm (see presets). Since 44.96.0 minimumReleaseAgeBuffer adds 30 minutes once a delay is set. ↗
  • cooldown.exclude.setting: No dedicated exclusion list. ↗
  • cooldown.exclude.patternSyntax: Glob and regex patterns since 38.0.0. ↗
  • cooldown.securityUpdates: The vulnerabilityAlerts defaults set minimumReleaseAge to null; a delay can still be set inside vulnerabilityAlerts. ↗
  • installScripts.defaultPolicy: Renovate disables package manager scripts when it updates lockfiles; the Mend-hosted app never runs them. ↗
  • installScripts.disableAll: The default; the self-hosted global allowScripts also defaults to false. ↗
  • extra.configLocation: detectConfigFile looks for each name in configFiles (lib/config/app-strings.ts) in the file list of the repository, whose paths start at the repository root, so a renovate.json in a subdirectory is never read. The configuration options page (docs/usage/configuration-options.md at 44.198.2): "Any config you define applies to the whole repository (e.g. if you have a monorepo)" and "Renovate always uses the config from the repository's default branch". ↗
  • extra.durationStrings: Values such as "3 days" or "1 hour", parsed by toMs (lib/util/pretty-time.ts). ↗
  • extra.minimumReleaseAgePresets: config:best-practices extends security:minimumReleaseAgeNpm, which sets minimumReleaseAge "3 days" for the npm datasource (see presets). ↗
  • extra.excludeRule: The documented opt-out: a packageRules entry with matchPackageNames and minimumReleaseAge: null. ↗
  • extra.securityUpdatesSetting: The vulnerabilityAlerts default sets minimumReleaseAge to null, so security updates skip the delay unless a value is set there. ↗
  • extra.excludeWildcards: matchPackageNames accepts globs (and regex) since 38.0.0. ↗
  • extra.ruleDescription: A human-readable description allowed inside any configuration object; depcurfew uses it to recognize the packageRules entry it manages. ↗
  • extra.packageJsonConfig: Deprecated: the configuration options page warns that support may be removed, and Renovate logs that using package.json is deprecated. ↗
  • extra.durationUnits: Units shown in the docs: minimumReleaseAge=1 hour on this page and "3 days" in the configuration options. The value is parsed by toMs (lib/util/pretty-time.ts), whose tests also accept 1 day, 1 week and combined values. ↗
  • extra.datasources: Datasource ids for matchDatasources: npm packages come from the npm datasource and Python packages from pypi, whose release timestamp is the earliest upload_time of the version's files. ↗
  • extra.negativeMatch: Patterns starting with ! are negative matches, and none of them may match; matchDatasources is matched this way (matchRegexOrGlobList in lib/util/package-rules/datasources.ts at 44.198.0), so ["!pypi"] covers every datasource but PyPI. ↗
  • extra.packageRuleMatching: A packageRules entry applies to a dependency when all of its matchers match. Within one matcher, at least one positive pattern must match when there are any, and no negative pattern (starting with !) may match, so a list of only negative patterns matches every name it does not exclude (docs/usage/string-pattern-matching.md and matchRegexOrGlobList in lib/util/string-match.ts). matchPackageNames, matchDepNames, matchDatasources and matchUpdateTypes all match this way; "*" alone matches everything. ↗
  • extra.packageRulesOrder: All packageRules are evaluated and later rules override options set by earlier ones; a matching rule overrides the top-level configuration ("Package rule config overrides any existing config", lib/util/package-rules/index.ts). Presets in extends are merged before the repository configuration, and packageRules is mergeable, so the rules of presets come first and the repository's rules after them, while a top-level option of the repository replaces the same option of a preset (resolveConfigPresets in lib/config/presets/index.ts and mergeChildConfig in lib/config/utils.ts). ↗
  • extra.delayedUpdateTypes: The update types that take minimumReleaseAge into account; digest and pinDigest only for some datasources, and pin, lockFileMaintenance, lockfileUpdate, rollback, bump and replacement not at all. ↗
  • extra.internalPresets: The built-in preset groups; a default preset is written :name or default:name. Other presets (github>, gitlab>, local>, npm packages and so on) are fetched from elsewhere. Among the built-in presets only the security:minimumReleaseAge* presets set minimumReleaseAge, each in a packageRules entry with matchDatasources for its datasource, and config:best-practices extends security:minimumReleaseAgeNpm (security.preset.ts and config.preset.ts in the same directory). ↗
  • extra.pypiPresets: Added in 44.8.0; config:best-practices does not include it (it includes only security:minimumReleaseAgeNpm). ↗
  • extra.pythonManagers: pep621 updates pyproject.toml with uv.lock (there is no uv manager) or pdm.lock; poetry updates pyproject.toml with poetry.lock; pipenv updates the Pipfile with Pipfile.lock; pip_requirements updates requirements files. pip-compile is off by default (lib/modules/manager/pep621/readme.md and pipenv/index.ts at 44.198.1). ↗
  • extra.passesDelayToManager: Since 44.34.0 Renovate passes POETRY_SOLVER_MIN_RELEASE_AGE (rounded up to whole days) to Poetry 2.4.0 and later. It does not pass the delay to uv or read exclude-newer. ↗
  • extra.missingTimestamp: minimumReleaseAgeBehaviour was added in 41.150.0; timestamp-optional still fails open. ↗
  • extra.independentOfPackageManager: Source code passes --before to npm only; pnpm, Yarn and Bun gates are not read. ↗

Dependabot

  • cooldown.setting: Per updates entry: default-days, semver-major-days, semver-minor-days, semver-patch-days, include, exclude. The semver-* keys apply only to SemVer ecosystems. ↗
  • cooldown.configFiles: On the default branch. ↗
  • cooldown.unit: Integer days; the allowed range is disputed (see dayRange). ↗
  • cooldown.availableSince: GA date on github.com; NuGet and Helm since 2025-07-29. ↗
  • cooldown.default: Version updates wait 3 days even without a cooldown block (GHES from 3.23); default-days is also 3 when cooldown is set without it. ↗
  • cooldown.securityUpdates: Cooldown exists only for version updates. dependabot-core also passes a zero age to npm, pnpm and Yarn for security updates, overriding repository gates. ↗
  • installScripts.allowList.setting: insecure-external-code-execution: allow exists only for bundler, mix and pip; nothing allows npm, pnpm, Yarn or Bun scripts. ↗
  • extra.configLocation: "You must store this file in the .github directory of your repository in the default branch (typically main), at .github/dependabot.yml or .github/dependabot.yaml" (github/docs, content/code-security/concepts/supply-chain-security/about-the-dependabot-yml-file.md, read 2026-11-01), so a .github/dependabot.yml in a subdirectory is not read. ↗
  • extra.directories: Required in each updates entry: directory for one directory of manifests, directories for a list; "Define directories relative to the root of the repository for most package managers"; "The directories key supports globbing and the wildcard character *. These features are not supported by the directory key." How a glob matches is not described further (github/docs, content/code-security/reference/supply-chain-security/dependabot-options-reference.md, read 2026-11-01). ↗
  • extra.cooldownKeys: Keys inside the cooldown block of an updates entry; the semver-* keys override default-days for that kind of update. ↗
  • extra.excludeWildcards: cooldown.exclude accepts * wildcards. ↗
  • extra.cooldownInclude: An include list in the cooldown block limits the cooldown to the dependencies it names (* wildcards, up to 150 entries): the example applies the cooldown to requests, numpy and names starting with pandas or django, and to cover all dependencies one omits include or uses "*". The other dependencies get no cooldown at all, not even the default 3 days: dependabot-core skips the cooldown for a dependency that the include list does not match (ReleaseCooldownOptions#included? with File.fnmatch? and CooldownCalculation.skip_cooldown? in common/lib/dependabot, commit 03892a3e8ee26d43eb96f31c5136c98a85d63b14). exclude wins over include. ↗
  • extra.ecosystems: The npm ecosystem covers npm, pnpm and Yarn; bun needs Bun 1.1.39 or later and deno needs Deno 2. pip covers pip, pip-compile, pipenv and Poetry (v2); uv has its own ecosystem (the table lists uv v0.11). Cooldown, including the semver-* keys, is supported for all of them. PDM is not listed: dependabot-core skips the dependencies of pyproject.toml when pdm.lock exists (python/lib/dependabot/python/file_parser/pyproject_files_parser.rb at v0.402.0). ↗
  • extra.maximumDays: The page says the number of cooldown days must be between 1 and 90; whether 0 is accepted is disputed (see dayRange). ↗

Environment

  • corepack.packageManagerField: Format name@x.y.z, optionally followed by +<hash algorithm>.<hash>; a URL to a .js or .tgz file is also allowed. setup-bun also reads bun@x from this key. ↗
  • corepack.devEnginesSince: Limited support at first; since 0.36.0 Corepack uses the devEngines.packageManager range when packageManager is missing. ↗
  • corepack.envFileSince: Corepack reads COREPACK_* keys from .corepack.env; since 0.36.0 only the closest file. COREPACK_ENV_FILE=0 turns it off. ↗
  • corepack.bundledWithNode: Node.js 25.0.0 stopped distributing Corepack; the Corepack README says it ships with Node.js from 14.19.0 up to (but not including) 25.0.0. ↗
  • corepack.package: Its published bin field (publishConfig.bin) declares yarn (./dist/yarn.js), yarnpkg, pnpm and pnpx next to corepack. corepack enable links them from the folder of the corepack executable (a relative symlink to dist/<name>.js on Linux and macOS, a cmd-shim on Windows; sources/commands/Enable.ts), so the package.json behind a yarn or pnpm shim is Corepack's, which says nothing about the version the shim runs. ↗
  • corepack.toolPackages: The npm packages Corepack installs each package manager from: npm; pnpm; yarn below 2.0.0 (Yarn Classic) and, with COREPACK_NPM_REGISTRY, @yarnpkg/cli-dist for Yarn 2 and later ("npmRegistry": { "package": "@yarnpkg/cli-dist", "bin": "bin/yarn.js" }). npm i -g @yarnpkg/cli-dist installs the same package, whose package.json declares bin yarn and carries the Yarn version (observed for 4.18.1). ↗
  • corepack.enableNpm: corepack enable [... name] creates shims for the package managers it is given, or for all of them without parameters: "Note that the npm shims will not be installed unless explicitly requested, as npm is currently distributed with Node.js through other means". So corepack enable npm makes npm the version Corepack picks (packageManager), while a bare corepack enable leaves the bundled npm. ↗
  • node.bundledNpm: The npm field of every release in index.json (fetched on 2026-11-01, sha256 6b27fe5766e87929d16ccdde05c516cbdd12200eab62b28d4b2f31d34e21481a), as the first release of each line that bundles each npm version; latest is the newest release of the line that day. Lines are the major version from 4 on and the minor version before (0.6 to 0.12, as Node.js numbered them then). The bundled npm never goes down within a line. Releases before 0.6.3 (0.1.14 to 0.5.10 and 0.6.0 to 0.6.2) have no npm field: they came without npm. Every release before 18.0.0 bundles npm 8.19.4 or earlier (16.20.2 has npm 8.19.4), so none of them has min-release-age, allowScripts or strict-allow-scripts. 27.0.0-alpha.0.0.0 (npm 12.1.0) is a prerelease and left out: no release bundles npm 12 yet, Node.js 22 stays on npm 10.9.x, 24 reaches npm 11.10 or later only from 24.14.1 (npm 11.10.x was skipped) and 25 ends at npm 11.12.1. ↗
  • node.releaseSchedule: start, lts and end of each line from 4 on, codenames in lower case as nvm and the node image tags write them. After end a line gets no more releases, security fixes included; the README says "Production applications should only use Active LTS or Maintenance LTS releases". From Node.js 27 every line first goes through an alpha phase (27 has "alpha": "2026-10-28" and no codename yet) and every line becomes LTS. ↗
  • node.nvmrc: The file must contain precisely one version followed by a newline; # starts a comment, and comments, blank lines and leading and trailing white space are ignored. node is the latest version and stable an alias for it; lts/* is the latest LTS and lts/<codename> the LTS releases of that line; the file may hold "any other string that nvm understands". nvm use looks for the file from the current directory upwards. lts/-n is not in the README but in nvm.sh at the same commit (https://github.com/nvm-sh/nvm/blob/5e38bc0caed342356ffd26eb21e0e9b995ac228b/nvm.sh, read 2026-11-01): nvm_normalize_lts, which nvm_alias calls for every alias (so also for the version of .nvmrc), matches "lts/-[123456789] | lts/-[123456789][0123456789]*", takes N from after the -, adds 1 and picks the N-th last entry of the lts alias directory, whose entries are the LTS codenames (alphabetical, which is release order) after *; so lts/-1 is the LTS line before the newest one, as in setup-node. When nothing matches it says "That many LTS releases do not exist yet." ↗
  • node.nodeVersionFile: nodenv uses the version in .node-version of the project directory. fnm reads .node-version and .nvmrc and writes the file with node --version, so a leading v occurs (github.com/Schniz/fnm/blob/07f13237ae208f531f8ea88d4cfde3160c22f99a/README.md); setup-node reads it through node-version-file. ↗
  • node.engines: engines.node is the range of Node.js versions the package works on. Unless engine-strict is set, it is advisory and only produces warnings when the package is installed as a dependency; it does not choose the Node.js that runs. setup-node reads it from package.json through node-version-file when volta.node and devEngines.runtime are missing, and picks the newest version that matches. ↗
  • node.inheritedVersionFiles: nvm: "nvm use et. al. will traverse directory structure upwards from the current directory looking for the .nvmrc file". nodenv: find_local_version_file in libexec/nodenv-version-file checks the directory for .node-version and then each parent up to the root (https://github.com/nodenv/nodenv/blob/5083e9819756c1a127a626a874703349f4ddb2e4/libexec/nodenv-version-file). asdf: the tool versions a .tool-versions file declares "will be used in that directory and any subdirectories" (https://github.com/asdf-vm/asdf/blob/master/docs/manage/configuration.md). Volta is not one of them: for_dir in crates/volta-core/src/project/mod.rs "will search ancestors to find a package.json and use that as the root of the project" and reads a parent package.json only through extends (https://github.com/volta-cli/volta/blob/main/crates/volta-core/src/project/mod.rs), so the volta key of the root package.json does not reach a project with a package.json of its own. ↗
  • docker.imageReference: splitDockerDomain canonicalizes ubuntu and docker.io/ubuntu as docker.io/library/ubuntu, and the legacy domain index.docker.io as docker.io. A reference without a tag means latest (docs/reference/commandline/image_tag.md in github.com/docker/cli at 8c8a77521af82b08bf3499cafb4c0e2e7b024936: "If the namespace is omitted, Docker defaults to library, the namespace reserved for Docker Official Images"). registry-1.docker.io is Docker Hub's registry itself: "DefaultRegistryHost is the hostname for the default (Docker Hub) registry used for pushing and pulling images", DefaultRegistryHost = "registry-1.docker.io" (https://github.com/moby/moby/blob/9aa9edfa787e08bb2da31694cd8a402876f32232/daemon/pkg/registry/config.go), so registry-1.docker.io/library/node is the same official image. ↗
  • docker.syntax: Instructions are not case-sensitive. A line that begins with # is a comment unless it is a parser directive; comment lines are removed also inside a continued instruction. Parser directives (syntax, escape, check; keys not case-sensitive) must come first: after a comment, an empty line or an instruction none is read. The escape character (\ by default, or `) at the end of a line continues the instruction; escaping is not performed in RUN except at the end of a line. ARG is the only instruction allowed before FROM, and FROM reads ARGs declared before the first FROM ($VAR, ${VAR}, ${VAR:-word}, ${VAR-word}, ${VAR:+word}, ${VAR+word}); --build-arg can change them. FROM [--platform=<platform>] <image>[:<tag>][@<digest>] [AS <name>]; a later FROM <name> starts from that stage. A here-document (RUN <<EOF) makes the following lines part of the instruction, up to the line with only the delimiter. Only RUN, COPY and ADD in shell form take here-documents, and only a whole word starts one: the parser splits the line into words with quotes kept (shlex.RawQuotes) and matches each word against ^(\d*)<<(-?)\s*([^<]*)$, so a quoted "a <<b" starts none (heredocsFromLine and reHeredoc in frontend/dockerfile/parser/parser.go at the same commit). ↗
  • docker.copySources: COPY and ADD without --from: "When copying source files from the build context, paths are interpreted as relative to the root of the context. Specifying a source path with a leading slash or one that navigates outside the build context, such as COPY ../something /something, automatically removes any parent directory navigation (../). Trailing slashes in the source path are also disregarded, making COPY something/ /something equivalent to COPY something /something." The ADD section says the same about ADD ../something. So /frontend/x, ../frontend/x and frontend/x are the same file, and frontend/.. is the root of the context. ↗
  • docker.environment: ENV <key>=<value> [<key>=<value>...]: "This value will be in the environment for all subsequent instructions in the build stage", and "A stage inherits any environment variables that were set using ENV by its parent stage or any ancestor". "Like command line parsing, quotes and backslashes can be used to include spaces within values". The alternative "ENV <key> <value>, omitting the =" sets one variable, and is discouraged. RUN commands see these variables, and ENV wins over an ARG of the same name. ↗
  • docker.dockerfileNames: "The default filename to use for a Dockerfile is Dockerfile, without a file extension." "A common convention is to name these <something>.Dockerfile", passed with --file. ↗
  • docker.containerfile: "A Containerfile uses the same syntax as a Dockerfile internally"; Buildah and Podman read Containerfile or Dockerfile. Docker does not read Containerfile by default. ↗
  • docker.nodeImage: The image downloads the official tarball of the release and extracts it unchanged; its only npm command is npm --version, and docs/BestPractices.md tells users to add RUN npm install --global npm@<version> to change npm. So node:<version> has exactly the npm that release bundles. The Alpine images extract the -musl tarball or build from source. Tags (stackbrew.js): X.Y.Z, X.Y, X and <codename>, each also with -<variant>, and -alpine and -slim for the default variants; the variants are those of versions.json, plus alpine and slim. Yarn 1 is in the images up to Node.js 25. ↗
  • docker.pythonImage: "our policy is now to use the pip version that is installed during the Python build (which is the version bundled in ensurepip), and to not support overriding it": Dockerfile-linux.template configures CPython --with-ensurepip and installs no other pip (only setuptools and wheel for 3.11). So python:<version> has the pip that CPython release bundles. Images built before 2024-09-07 installed pip with get-pip.py, at the version ensurepip had. Tags (generate-stackbrew-library.sh): X.Y.Z and X.Y, each with -<variant>; -slim and -alpine mean the default suite. ↗
  • devEngines: Each entry is an object or a list of objects with name (required), version and onFail (warn, error by default, or ignore). Corepack and the setup actions read it too. ↗
  • devEnginesSince: npm version that introduced devEngines. ↗
