  • volta.manifest: Also extends: a path to another JSON file with a volta key. pnpm support is experimental and needs VOLTA_FEATURE_PNPM=1. ↗
  • volta.maintained: The README says Volta is unmaintained and recommends migrating to mise. ↗
  • asdf.toolVersionsFile: One tool per line followed by one or more versions separated by spaces (fallbacks); # starts a comment. ↗
  • asdf.plugins: Short names in the asdf plugin index; there is no plugin named node. python, uv and poetry checked on 2026-10-30 (danhper/asdf-python, asdf-community/asdf-uv, asdf-community/asdf-poetry). ↗
  • githubActions.setupNode: Current major v7. node-version-file reads package.json, .nvmrc, .node-version, .tool-versions or mise.toml; node-version wins when both are set. ↗
  • githubActions.setupNodeVersions: Tag v7.1.0. node-version takes semver (22, 24.8.0, >=10.15.0, 12.x), the NVM LTS syntax (lts/jod, lts/*, lts/-n) and latest, current, node or * for the latest release. A matching version in the runner's tool cache is used first, so a major version such as 24 may give an older release of the line unless check-latest is set. ↗
  • githubActions.setupNodeVersionFile: node-version-file accepts .nvmrc, .node-version, .tool-versions, mise.toml or package.json, relative to GITHUB_WORKSPACE. In package.json it checks volta.node, then the devEngines.runtime entry named node, then engines.node, then volta.extends. getNodeVersionFromFile in src/util.ts tries JSON, then TOML tools.node, then the first line that matches the pattern, then the whole text trimmed. ↗
  • githubActions.jobContainer: Read from the page source in github/docs (data/reusables/actions/jobs/section-running-jobs-in-a-container.md and section-running-jobs-in-a-container-image.md, main on 2026-11-01): "Use jobs.<job_id>.container to create a container to run any steps in a job that don't already specify a container"; "If you do not set a container, all steps will run directly on the host specified by runs-on"; "When you only specify a container image, you can omit the image keyword" (container: node:18); jobs.<job_id>.container.image is "the Docker image to use as the container to run the action", and container.env sets variables in the container. So the run steps of such a job use the Node.js and npm of that image, until a setup step installs another. ↗
  • githubActions.environmentKeys: Read from the page source in github/docs (content/actions/reference/workflows-and-actions/workflow-syntax.md and data/reusables/repositories/actions-env-var-note.md, main on 2026-11-01): env is "A map of variables that are available to the steps of all jobs in the workflow", jobs.<job_id>.env "A map of variables that are available to all steps in the job", and jobs.<job_id>.steps[*].env "Sets variables for steps to use in the runner environment"; for the same name GitHub "uses the most specific variable": the step's over the job's over the workflow's. ↗
  • githubActions.pnpmActionSetup: Current major v6. Without version the action reads devEngines.packageManager or packageManager from package.json (package_json_file). ↗
  • githubActions.pnpmSetup: For pnpm 11 and later. Without version it reads devEngines.packageManager or packageManager; runtime takes <name>@<version> for node, bun or deno. ↗
  • githubActions.setupBun: Current major v2. Without bun-version it reads packageManager (bun@x), then engines.bun, then installs latest. ↗
  • githubActions.setupDeno: Current major v2. deno-version defaults to 2.x; deno-version-file reads .dvmrc or .tool-versions. ↗
  • githubActions.setupPython: Current version v7.1.0. Without python-version it reads .python-version; python-version wins over python-version-file. pip-version (major[.minor][.patch], CPython only) since v6.0.0. Python comes with pip, but the step alone does not show that pip installs the project. ↗
  • githubActions.setupUv: Current version v10.3.3, with no moving v10 tag. Without version or version-file it reads required-version from uv.toml, then pyproject.toml, in the working directory, and installs the latest uv when neither has one. ↗
  • githubActions.installPoetry: A third-party action (current v1.4.2); version defaults to latest, so without it the Poetry version is not pinned. ↗
  • githubActions.actionsPoetry: A third-party action (current v4.0.0); poetry-version defaults to latest. ↗
  • githubActions.codeScanningUpload: The example workflow on this page needs security-events: write in every repository and actions: read with contents: read only in private ones. The inputs come from upload-sarif/action.yml in github/codeql-action (current major v4, v4.39.1 checked); category tells analyses of the same tool and commit apart. ↗
  • githubActions.stopCommands: Lines between the two are logged without being processed as workflow commands. The token must be randomly generated and unique for each run. Read from the page source in github/docs. ↗
  • githubActions.annotations: escapeData and escapeProperty in @actions/core: the message and property values replace %, CR and LF; property values also replace : and , because properties are separated by commas. The commands and their file, line and title parameters are on https://docs.github.com/en/actions/reference/workflows-and-actions/workflow-commands (line counts from 1). ↗
  • githubActions.annotationLimits: 10 error, 10 warning and 10 notice annotations per step; 50 annotations per job, summed over all steps. Annotations over the limit are not shown. ↗
  • githubActions.checkRuns: Read from the sources of this page (#create-a-check-run, #update-a-check-run, #list-check-runs-for-a-git-reference), the REST description github/rest-api-description (descriptions/api.github.com/api.github.com.json, commit f9ed74230d54847b5ca00a1343e8e349e858b8fb) and github/docs (commit 33b1f4a6fec2d9b6fd3d374afa56bcd9f0875b57); content/rest/checks/runs.md is generated from the REST description. POST /repos/{owner}/{repo}/check-runs ("Create a check run") needs name and head_sha and takes external_id, "A reference for the run on the integrator's system"; PATCH /repos/{owner}/{repo}/check-runs/{check_run_id} ("Update a check run") takes the same fields but head_sha, with only summary required in output, and "OAuth apps and personal access tokens (classic) cannot use this endpoint"; GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}/commits/{ref}/check-runs ("List check runs for a Git reference", ref a SHA, heads/BRANCH or tags/TAG) takes check_name ("Returns check runs with the specified name"), filter (latest, the default, or all: "latest returns the most recent check runs"), per_page (at most 100) and app_id, and answers {total_count, check_runs}, each with id, name, external_id (nullable), app (with slug, not required) and output.annotations_count. Fine-grained permissions (src/github-apps/data/fpt-2022-11-28/server-to-server-permissions.json in github/docs): the list needs Checks read, update and create need Checks write; "write includes read" (github-token-scope-descriptions.md). output.annotations: "maxItems": 50 per request, "To create more than 50 annotations, you have to make multiple requests to the Update a check run endpoint. Each time you update the check run, annotations are appended"; each annotation requires path, start_line, end_line (lines start at 1), annotation_level (notice, warning or failure) and message ("The maximum size is 64 KB"), with an optional title ("The maximum size is 255 characters"). No total per check run is documented; the same description says "GitHub Actions are limited to 10 warning annotations and 10 error annotations per step" without saying whether that binds check runs created through the API (uncertain). Not documented either: whether only the app that created a run may update it, and the app slug of runs created with the workflow token. A check suite holds the runs of one GitHub App for one commit (using-the-rest-api-to-interact-with-checks.md), and GitHub keeps at most 1000 runs of the same name per suite. status completed needs a conclusion (success, neutral, failure and others); output.title and output.summary (Markdown, maxLength 65535) show in the Checks tab; only GitHub Apps can create check runs, which the workflow token is. checks: write "permits an action to create a check run" (data/reusables/actions/github-token-scope-descriptions.md in github/docs, which links the permissions with apiVersion=2022-11-28); a pull request from a fork gets a read-only token unless the repository sends write tokens to such workflows (workflow-syntax.md, How permissions are calculated). For pull_request, GITHUB_SHA is the "Last merge commit on the GITHUB_REF branch" (events-that-trigger-workflows.md), so the check goes on pull_request.head.sha from the payload file GITHUB_EVENT_PATH names (webhook-pull-request-opened schema; variables.md for GITHUB_API_URL, GITHUB_REPOSITORY and GITHUB_EVENT_PATH). ↗
  • githubActions.stepSummary: GitHub flavored Markdown appended to the file named by the variable; at most 1 MiB per step (over that the upload fails with an error annotation), and at most 20 step summaries are shown per job. ↗
  • npmRegistry.api: GET /{package} returns the full document by default: dist-tags maps tags to versions (every package has latest), versions maps versions to version objects, time maps every version to its publish time, and a version object carries scripts (from its package.json) and deprecated. The abbreviated document (Accept: application/vnd.npm.install-v1+json) has no time field and adds hasInstallScript. Scoped names are escaped with %2f (escapedName in github.com/npm/npm-package-arg); the registry answered the same document for @types/node and @types%2fnode on 2026-10-29. Install scripts: preinstall, install and postinstall run for a dependency, and npm runs node-gyp rebuild as install when the package has binding.gyp and no install or preinstall script (github.com/npm/cli/blob/v12.2.1/docs/lib/content/using-npm/scripts.md); binding.gyp is not visible in the document. ↗
  • pypiIndex.api: GET /simple/<project>/ with Accept: application/vnd.pypi.simple.v1+json returns name, files, meta, versions and project-status; the project is matched case-insensitively with _, - and . considered equal (index-api.md in pypi/warehouse, the source of docs.pypi.org/api/index-api/, which recommends the JSON form for new integrations; the JSON API of /pypi/<project>/json marks its releases key deprecated). A file has filename, url, hashes, size, an optional upload-time (yyyy-mm-ddThh:mm:ss.ffffffZ, UTC) and an optional yanked (true or a reason string). Every file belongs to a version listed in versions, but the response does not say which: the version is part of the file name. On 2026-10-30 pypi.org answered api-version 1.4 for requests and 404 for a name it does not know. ↗
  • pypiIndex.projectStatus: A project has exactly one status, active when none is given. quarantined: considered generally unsafe (for example malware), and the index must not offer any of its files. archived: no new uploads, existing files stay; installers may warn. deprecated: obsolete, perhaps superseded by another project; installers may warn. ↗
  • claudeCode.hooks: A PreToolUse command hook gets the event as JSON on stdin and decides by printing one JSON object and exiting 0: deny prevents the call and shows the reason to Claude, ask prompts the user (also in auto mode), deny wins over ask. The PowerShell tool passes its command in tool_input.command with the same fields as the Bash tool. A matcher of names separated by | matches each of them exactly. With args set, command runs as an executable without a shell; on Windows it must resolve to a real executable such as a .exe, not a .cmd or .bat shim from node_modules/.bin. Without onFailure, a hook that fails to start, crashes or times out lets the call through. The if filter is best-effort; the docs recommend the permission system for hard allow or deny rules. ↗
  • claudeCode.sessionStart: SessionStart runs when a session starts or resumes; its matcher startup fires for a new session (also resume, clear, compact and fork). Hooks run in the background, but "Claude's first response still waits for the hooks to finish", and a prompt sent meanwhile does not reach Claude until they finish. A command hook may be shell form (command with $CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR, the repository root). With onFailure set to "block" (#block-the-action-when-a-hook-fails), a PreToolUse hook whose script is missing blocks the call ("failed; blocking because onFailure is "block"" followed by node's error output, in the example "PreToolUse:Bash hook error: [node ${CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR}/.claude/hooks/check-command.js]: failed; blocking because onFailure is "block"" and "Error: Cannot find module '/path/to/project/.claude/hooks/check-command.js'", re-read 2026-11-01): node starts, cannot load the script and exits with 1, which counts as a failure; without it, the same missing script is a non-blocking error and the command runs. In cloud sessions (https://code.claude.com/docs/en/cloud-environments#setup-scripts) a setup script is a Bash script that runs as root before Claude Code launches and is skipped when a cached environment exists; SessionStart hooks of the repository's .claude/settings.json run in local and cloud sessions (in a session with one repository, outside a project thread), user-level ~/.claude/settings.json hooks do not reach the cloud. The docs recommend a SessionStart hook for project setup such as npm install. ↗
  • claudeCode.shellTools: Tool names are the exact strings of hook matchers. Bash executes shell commands. PowerShell executes PowerShell commands natively; hooks get its command in tool_input.command with the same fields as Bash, and the docs say to match Bash|PowerShell in hooks that inspect shell commands: on Windows, wherever the PowerShell tool is enabled, Claude treats PowerShell as the primary shell, and without Git Bash it is enabled automatically and the Bash tool is not registered at all (also https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks#powershell). On Windows with Git Bash it is on by default for claude.ai and Console accounts, CLAUDE_CODE_USE_POWERSHELL_TOOL=0 turns it off, and Claude Code runs pwsh.exe or else powershell.exe 5.1. Monitor runs a command in the background and feeds its output lines back to Claude, with the same permission rules as Bash; on Windows it is available only with Git Bash; a WebSocket watch takes a ws input in place of command. ↗
  • claudeCode.commandlessInputs: "A WebSocket watch takes a ws input in place of command, and a single Monitor call can't combine the two"; ws has url (a ws:// or wss:// URL) and optional protocols. Opening a WebSocket has its own approval prompt. ↗
  • mcp.revisions: Modern: revisions that carry version, identity and capabilities on every request (2026-07-28 and later); legacy: revisions that open a session with the initialize handshake (2025-11-25 and earlier). A dual-era server may serve both on one stdio process: a request with the modern per-request _meta is served statelessly, an initialize request selects legacy semantics for the process. docs/docs.json lists 2026-07-28 (latest), 2025-11-25, 2025-06-18, 2025-03-26 and 2024-11-05; schema/2026-07-28/schema.ts and schema/draft/schema.ts both say LATEST_PROTOCOL_VERSION = "2026-07-28". ↗
  • mcp.lifecycle: If the server supports the requested protocolVersion it must answer with the same one, otherwise with another version it supports, which should be its latest; a client that cannot use it disconnects (the same wording in 2024-11-05). After the result the client sends notifications/initialized. The initialize request must not be cancelled and, in 2025-03-26, must not be part of a batch. ping (basic/utilities/ping.mdx) is answered promptly with an empty result and may be sent before initialization. 2026-07-28 removes initialize, notifications/initialized and ping (docs/specification/2026-07-28/changelog.mdx). ↗
  • mcp.stateless: Every 2026-07-28 request carries params._meta with io.modelcontextprotocol/protocolVersion and io.modelcontextprotocol/clientCapabilities (required; a request missing either is rejected with -32602) and optionally io.modelcontextprotocol/clientInfo; servers should put io.modelcontextprotocol/serverInfo in the _meta of every result, and every result must carry resultType ("complete" for a finished one; clients read an absent one as complete). Servers must implement server/discover (server/discover.mdx: supportedVersions, capabilities, instructions, serverInfo in _meta). server/utilities/caching.mdx: results of server/discover and tools/list must carry ttlMs (>= 0) and cacheScope ("public" suits lists of tools that are the same for all users). An unsupported version gets -32022 with data { supported, requested } (basic/versioning.mdx). Self-reported identity must not change behavior. ↗
  • mcp.tools: A server with tools declares the tools capability and answers tools/list (tools in a deterministic order that does not vary per connection) and tools/call ({ name, arguments }; result content, optional structuredContent and isError). Errors of the tool itself, including input values the model can correct, belong in the result with isError: true; an unknown tool or a request that does not fit the schema is a protocol error (-32602). A tool that returns structuredContent should also return the same JSON in a text block. Tool names should be 1 to 128 characters of letters, digits, _, - and . . Annotations (readOnlyHint, openWorldHint and others) are hints that clients must treat as untrusted. Annotations arrived in 2025-03-26 (changelog.mdx), title and structured output in 2025-06-18 (changelog.mdx); in 2025-11-25 and earlier structuredContent is an object. ↗
  • mcp.cancellation: The client cancels an in-flight request with the notification notifications/cancelled and params { requestId, reason? }; the server should stop the work as soon as practical and must not send any further message for that request, and may ignore an unknown or finished request (also basic/transports/stdio.mdx). The same notification exists in the legacy revisions (2025-11-25/basic/utilities/cancellation.mdx). ↗
  • mcp.errorCodes: PARSE_ERROR, INVALID_REQUEST, METHOD_NOT_FOUND, INVALID_PARAMS, INTERNAL_ERROR and UNSUPPORTED_PROTOCOL_VERSION. -32601 is for a method the server does not implement. basic/index.mdx: request ids are strings or integers and never null; notifications have no id and get no response; an error response has the id of its request except when it could not be read, and the 2025-11-25 and 2026-07-28 schemas make id optional in an error response; codes from -32000 to -32019 are legacy and other codes from -32020 to -32099 that the specification does not define must not be used. ↗
  • mcp.errorId: JSONRPCErrorResponse has id?: RequestId in the 2025-11-25 schema (also in schema/2026-07-28/schema.ts); the schemas of 2025-06-18 and 2024-11-05 have JSONRPCError with a required id: RequestId (string | number), and basic/index.mdx of 2025-06-18 says responses must include the same id as their request and request ids must not be null. Neither says what an error response for a request whose id cannot be read carries; JSON-RPC 2.0 uses null there (jsonrpc.org was not reachable to verify it). ↗
  • mcp.stdio: The server reads JSON-RPC messages from stdin and writes them to stdout, delimited by newlines and without embedded newlines; it must not write anything to stdout that is not a valid MCP message, may log UTF-8 to stderr and should exit promptly when stdin is closed. Batches: 2025-03-26 (basic/index.mdx) says implementations must support receiving JSON-RPC batches; 2025-06-18 removed batching (changelog.mdx), and later revisions have no batch message. ↗
  • mcp.clients.claudeCode: claude mcp add [options] <name> -- <command> [args...]: everything after -- goes to the server untouched. Scopes: local (the default, ~/.claude.json), project (.mcp.json in the project root, shared through the repository; Claude Code asks before using its servers) and user. .mcp.json has a top-level mcpServers object of entries with command, args and env; an entry without type is a stdio server. Claude Code sets CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR to the project root in the environment of the server, not in its own, so ${VAR} expansion in command or args of .mcp.json needs a default such as ${CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR:-.}. To stop a stdio server it sends SIGINT, then SIGTERM after 100 ms and SIGKILL after a further 400 ms (macOS, Linux); on Windows it closes stdin and ends the process tree after 300 ms. ↗
  • mcp.clients.claudeDesktop: The page "Connect to local MCP servers" of modelcontextprotocol.io: Claude Desktop reads mcpServers (entries with command, args and env) from claude_desktop_config.json. It documents no working directory and no variables, and its troubleshooting asks for absolute paths. The stderr of a server goes to mcp-server-<name>.log in ~/Library/Logs/Claude (macOS) or %APPDATA%\Claude\logs (Windows). ↗
  • mcp.clients.vscode: The source of code.visualstudio.com/docs/agents/reference/mcp-configuration. .vscode/mcp.json (in the workspace or the user profile) has a top-level servers object; a stdio server needs type "stdio" and command, and takes args, cwd (defaults to the workspace folder when run in a workspace), env and envFile; predefined variables such as ${workspaceFolder} work in the configuration. VS Code also reads the portable .mcp.json with mcpServers at the workspace root, without cwd. code --add-mcp with a JSON object of a name and the server configuration adds the server to the user profile. ↗
  • git.ignore: git status and git add skip untracked files that match a pattern from a .gitignore in the same directory or a parent (deeper files override higher ones, and within one file the last matching pattern decides), then from $GIT_COMMON_DIR/info/exclude (.git/info/exclude outside linked worktrees), then from core.excludesFile. Files already tracked are not affected. Patterns: # comments, ! negation, a leading or middle / anchors the pattern to the directory of its .gitignore, a trailing / matches directories only, * and ? do not match /, **/, /** and /**/ match any number of directories; a file below an ignored directory cannot be re-included. The tracked files are the entries of $GIT_DIR/index (Documentation/gitformat-index.adoc at v2.56.0: versions 2, 3 and 4). ↗
  • git.ignoreCase: core.ignoreCase turns on workarounds for file systems that are not case sensitive (APFS, HFS+, FAT, NTFS); it defaults to false, but git clone and git init probe the file system and set it to true when appropriate, so a repository cloned on Windows or macOS has it. With it, git matches .gitignore patterns and looks up the index without regard to case: match_basename and match_pathname in dir.c compare with fspathncmp and fnmatch_icase_mem, which fold case (strncasecmp, WM_CASEFOLD) when repo_ignore_case is set, and environment.c reads core.ignorecase (both at v2.56.0). Section and variable names are case-insensitive, true is yes, on, true or 1 in any case, and a variable without = <value> is true (Documentation/config.adoc at v2.56.0). ↗
  • git.repositoryEntry: "A submodule is a repository embedded inside another repository", with its own history; "the repository it is embedded in is called a superproject". Its git directory is "located under the $GIT_DIR/modules/ directory of its superproject", with "a .git file at the root of the submodule's working directory pointing to" it, and the superproject tracks it only "via a gitlink entry in the tree" with the commit it expects. So a directory with a .git entry of its own (a directory, or the file of a submodule) holds the files of another repository. ↗
  • windows.commandShims: cmd-shim is "the cmd-shim used in npm to create executable scripts on Windows" (README): for each program of a package it writes <name>.cmd, which runs the program with %* (so cmd.exe reads the arguments again), <name>.ps1 for PowerShell and a sh script. npm ships the same files for itself: bin/npm.cmd, bin/npm.ps1, bin/npx.cmd and bin/npx.ps1 in github.com/npm/cli at v12.2.1, the .cmd files ending in "%NODE_EXE%" "%NPM_CLI_JS%" %*. ↗
  • windows.batchArguments: about_Parsing (the source of https://learn.microsoft.com/powershell/module/microsoft.powershell.core/about/about_parsing): on Windows, arguments for batch files are passed as raw command-line strings to cmd.exe, and untrusted input should be passed another way. In the Windows mode of $PSNativeCommandArgumentPassing, the default on Windows since PowerShell 7.3, files ending in .bat or .cmd (also cmd.exe, cscript.exe, wscript.exe, .js, .vbs and .wsf) keep the legacy argument passing of Windows PowerShell 5.1. ↗
  • python.nameNormalization: Names are lowercased and every run of ., - or _ becomes a single -, before lookups and comparisons (PEP 503): Friendly.Bard and friendly_bard are the same package. Read from the page source in pypa/packaging.python.org. ↗
  • python.namePattern: The specification gives ^([A-Z0-9]|[A-Z0-9][A-Z0-9._-]*[A-Z0-9])\Z run with re.IGNORECASE (PEP 508). ↗
  • python.projectFile: The configuration file of a Python project, in the project root; tools keep their own settings in its [tool.<name>] tables. ↗
  • python.wheelExtension: A wheel is a ZIP archive with the .whl extension; installing it unpacks files and runs no build code. Read from the page source in pypa/packaging.python.org. ↗
  • python.sourceDistributionExtensions: ARCHIVE_EXTENSIONS of pip without .whl: the archives pip accepts from an index besides wheels (index/package_finder.py rejects any other extension), all of them built when installed. A standard sdist is named {name}-{version}.tar.gz with the name normalized (PEP 625, source-distribution-format.rst); older uploads use the other extensions and names that are not normalized. ↗
  • python.bundledPip: _PIP_VERSION in Lib/ensurepip/__init__.py at every final tag from v3.9.0 to v3.15.2 (github.com/python/cpython/blob/v<X.Y.Z>/...; the latest tags are v3.9.25 0bbaf5de, v3.10.22 acb4dc12, v3.11.18 26badd80, v3.12.16 2c2462af, v3.13.18 0d4bd512, v3.14.10 b703b2c5 and v3.15.2 5e58eaad), as the first release of each line that bundles each pip version. Two-part values such as 24.0 are written 24.0.0. 3.12 and older never bundle pip 26; pip 26.1 or later comes with 3.13.14, 3.14.5 and 3.15.0. ↗
  • python.hatch: Hatch reads its project configuration from [tool.hatch] of pyproject.toml or from hatch.toml. It has no setting of its own for a release-age delay, building from source or dependency sources; its environments install with uv (installer = "uv", the default of its internal environments) or pip (docs/how-to/environment/select-installer.md), and uv reads [tool.uv] of the project under Hatch too (tested on 2026-10-30 with Hatch 1.18.2 and uv 0.13.6). ↗
  • python.virtualEnvironment: Creating virtual environments: venv "places a pyvenv.cfg file in it with a home key" (in the target directory) and "also creates a bin (or Scripts on Windows) subdirectory". How venvs work: "A virtual environment may be "activated" using a script in its binary directory (bin on POSIX; Scripts on Windows)"; the table of activation commands: POSIX bash/zsh source <venv>/bin/activate, fish source <venv>/bin/activate.fish, csh/tcsh source <venv>/bin/activate.csh, pwsh <venv>/bin/Activate.ps1; Windows cmd.exe <venv>\Scripts\activate.bat, PowerShell <venv>\Scripts\Activate.ps1. Activation prepends the binary directory to PATH and sets VIRTUAL_ENV. Lib/venv/__init__.py at v3.14.0 writes pyvenv.cfg as os.path.join(context.env_dir, "pyvenv.cfg") and installs the scripts of the common directory and of os.name. ↗
  • python.interpreter: python [-bBdEhiIOPqRsSuvVWx?] [-c command | -m module-name | script | - ] [args]: -m and -c end the interpreter options, and what follows goes to the module or the command; -W arg, -X option and --check-hash-based-pycs mode take a value, the other options are flags. One-letter options may be grouped after one dash: _PyOS_GetOpt in Python/getopt.c (v3.14.0, SHORT_OPTS "bBc:dEhiIm:OPqRsStuvVW:xX:?") reads them one by one, and c, m, W and X take the rest of the word or the next word, so python3 -Im pip runs pip. pip documents running itself as python -m pip, and as py -m pip with the Windows launcher (github.com/pypa/pip/blob/26.2.2/docs/html/cli/pip_install.rst). ↗

To review

Uncertain facts rest on incomplete official sources; disputed facts have official sources that disagree.

npm · installScripts.allowList.format ❓ uncertain

The exact entry grammar (no semver ranges) is stated in accepted RFC 0054; the shipped docs describe entries only in prose.

  • Object keyed by name, name@version or name@v1 || v2 with true (allow) or false (deny); npm install-scripts approve writes pinned name@version entries · source, verified 2026-10-28.

The release notes add --allow-git in 11.9.0; the GitHub changelog says it was introduced in 11.10.0.

  • Variant 2: 11.10.0 · source, verified 2026-10-28.

The 11.14.0 release notes add the flag; the GitHub changelog dates it to 11.15.0, which also moved enforcement into the resolver. Treat 11.15.0 as the safe minimum.

  • Variant 2: 11.15.0 · source, verified 2026-10-28.

npm · extra.lockfileInstalls ❓ uncertain

Inferred: npm ci does not list min-release-age or before among its options, and a user report (npm/cli#9277) describes it installing a version younger than the window.

pnpm · cooldown.cliFlag ❓ uncertain

No dedicated flag is documented for install or add; release notes mention "a CLI flag" without syntax. Environment variable: PNPM_CONFIG_MINIMUM_RELEASE_AGE (pnpm 11+), npm_config_minimum_release_age (pnpm 10).

No setting restricts file: or link: dependencies (concluded from the full settings list); blockExoticSubdeps treats local paths and workspace links as trusted.

pnpm · sources.directory.setting ❓ uncertain

No setting restricts file: or link: dependencies (concluded from the full settings list); blockExoticSubdeps treats local paths and workspace links as trusted.

pnpm · project.workspacesObjectKey ❓ uncertain

The docs show packages only as a list; concluded from the absence of an object form.

pnpm · user.unknownKeys ❓ uncertain

pnpm 11 drops config.yaml keys it does not accept (unknown, project-only or not camelCase) and prints a warning that names them; pnpm 12 warns about keys its port of isConfigFileKey rejects, but drops blockExoticSubdeps without a word. What pnpm 10 does with an unknown key in rc was not checked.

  • warn · source, verified 2026-10-31.

pnpm · user.invalidValueFails ❓ uncertain

Read from code: pnpm 11.28.10 stops when the global config.yaml cannot be read (readGlobalYamlConfig) or applied (applyGlobalYamlConfig), except in commands that skip unreadable settings. Which values fail, and what pnpm 10 (rc) and pnpm 12 do, was not checked; true is the safe assumption.

  • true · source, verified 2026-10-31.

pnpm · extra.perProjectNpmrc ❓ uncertain

Read from code; no documentation or test covers it. No official source says this combination turns minimumReleaseAge off.

    11.1.3+: true · source, verified 2026-10-28.

Yarn (Berry) · user.invalidValueFails ❓ uncertain

Read from code at 4.18.1: parseValue errors are rethrown with the file name while the configuration loads, so a value Yarn cannot parse, such as enableScripts: maybe or a malformed duration, stops every command, also from the home file. On 4.10.x a duration string such as 7d silently had no effect instead (durationStrings). Not tested.

  • true · source, verified 2026-10-31.

Yarn (Berry) · user.weakening ❓ uncertain

Read from code (resolveRcFiles, the same since 4.0.0; not on yarnpkg.com, and no test was read): lists from every .yarnrc.yml are joined, so home entries add to a project's list unless the project uses the undocumented { onConflict: reset } form; objects such as npmScopes merge key by key; for single values the file closest to the project wins. Since 4.17.0 a scope listed in the home npmScopes only for its registry gets the default gate of 1d instead of the project's gate (perScopeGate). A .yarnrc.yml in any folder between the root and the project merges the same way. enableScripts is a switch that turns a protection off: Yarn reads every value as text (FAILSAFE_SCHEMA, see extra.yamlMergeKeys) and parseBoolean of miscUtils.ts takes true and 1 as true and false and 0 as false and stops on any other value, after replacing ${VARIABLE} references; depcurfew counts every value but false and 0 as on.

  • {"key":"approvedGitRepositories","values":"any","effect":"approves these git repositories in every project, on top of the project's own list"}, {"key":"npmPreapprovedPackages","values":"any","effect":"exempts these packages from the age gate in every project, on top of the project's own list"}, {"key":"enableScripts","values":{"except":[false,0,"false","0"]},"effect":"runs dependency build scripts in every project whose .yarnrc.yml does not set enableScripts"}, {"key":"npmMinimalAgeGate","values":[0],"effect":"turns the age gate off in every project whose .yarnrc.yml does not set it"}, {"key":"npmScopes.*.npmMinimalAgeGate","values":[0],"effect":"turns the age gate off for that scope in every project"} · source, verified 2026-10-31.

Yarn (Berry) · user.variables ⚠️ disputed

The yarnrc docs say environment variables cannot set lists, but parseValue splits a string on commas for list settings, so by the code YARN_APPROVED_GIT_REPOSITORIES and YARN_NPM_PREAPPROVED_PACKAGES work. The safe choice is to treat both as effective.

  • Variant 1: {"name":"YARN_ENABLE_SCRIPTS","anyCase":true,"values":{"except":["false","0"]},"effect":"runs dependency build scripts, whatever any .yarnrc.yml says"}, {"name":"YARN_NPM_MINIMAL_AGE_GATE","anyCase":true,"values":"any","effect":"replaces npmMinimalAgeGate from every .yarnrc.yml"}, {"name":"YARN_APPROVED_GIT_REPOSITORIES","anyCase":true,"values":"any","effect":"replaces approvedGitRepositories from every .yarnrc.yml (a comma-separated list)"}, {"name":"YARN_NPM_PREAPPROVED_PACKAGES","anyCase":true,"values":"any","effect":"replaces npmPreapprovedPackages from every .yarnrc.yml (a comma-separated list)"} · source, verified 2026-10-31. A variable starting with yarn_ in any case sets the camel-cased setting before the rc files are read, and they cannot change it (Configuration.find and use; YARN_IGNORE_SCRIPTS is ignored); a list setting takes a comma-separated string (parseValue); enableScripts accepts true or 1 (parseBoolean), false or 0 turn it off, and any other value stops Yarn, so every value but false and 0 counts as on.
  • Variant 2: {"name":"YARN_ENABLE_SCRIPTS","anyCase":true,"values":{"except":["false","0"]},"effect":"runs dependency build scripts, whatever any .yarnrc.yml says"}, {"name":"YARN_NPM_MINIMAL_AGE_GATE","anyCase":true,"values":"any","effect":"replaces npmMinimalAgeGate from every .yarnrc.yml"} · source, verified 2026-10-31. Most settings "can also be defined through environment variables (at least for the simpler ones; arrays and objects aren't supported yet)", and such values "will overwrite any that might have been defined in the RC files".

Yarn (Berry) · extra.yarnPath ❓ uncertain

yarn set version saves the release as .yarn/releases/yarn-<version>.cjs and writes a relative yarnPath to it. Yarn 2 and later read yarnPath (an absolute path setting, resolved against the folder of the .yarnrc.yml that sets it) from every .yarnrc.yml, the home file included, before the project is found (Configuration.find, pickPrimaryCoreFields), and run node <yarnPath> instead of themselves (runYarnPath in packages/yarnpkg-cli/sources/lib.ts); a nearer .yarnrc.yml with its own yarnPath, YARN_YARN_PATH or YARN_IGNORE_PATH changes that. The file name is only a hint: a URL or a file given to yarn set version gives other names (yarn-remote.cjs), a hand-written yarnPath may name any file, and the version inside the bundle is minified, so depcurfew uses the name only when it is yarn-<exact version>.cjs and the file exists.

  • {"key":"yarnPath","releaseFile":"yarn-<version>.cjs","variables":["YARN_YARN_PATH","YARN_IGNORE_PATH"]} · source, verified 2026-11-01.

Yarn (Berry) · extra.berryLockfileMarker ❓ uncertain

Read from code: Yarn 2+ writes a __metadata key into yarn.lock and treats a lockfile without it but with "yarn lockfile v1" as Yarn Classic.

  • __metadata · source, verified 2026-10-28.

Deno · user.variables ❓ uncertain

Only these two spellings are read (min_release_age_days_from_env); 0 turns the default 24 hours off. Poetry · extra.excludeFormat ⚠️ disputed

The 2.5.1 docs give the type string, a comma-separated list; the docs on main (pull request #11087, not released) give array and say to use a TOML array in poetry.toml.   • Variant 1: comma-separated string · source, verified 2026-10-30.

  • null · source, verified 2026-10-30.

Pipenv · user.variables ❓ uncertain

PIPENV_RESOLVER is not documented. The code ranks it above the resolver key of the Pipfile ("CLI/caller > env > current project's Pipfile > default"), and the pure-python backend locked the newest versions despite cool-down-period when chosen in the Pipfile (extra.resolverBypass, tested on 2026-10-30); the variable itself was not tested. PIPENV_PYPI_MIRROR: "When the index does not provide upload-time metadata (e.g. most private mirrors) the setting is accepted but has no filtering effect" (docs/pipfile.md). PIP_* variables are merged over the environment of the pip subprocess (pipenv/utils/processes.py), so they still reach it while pip.conf does not; pipenv/routines/install.py also reads PIP_NO_BINARY.

  • {"name":"PIPENV_RESOLVER","values":["pure-python"],"effect":"selects the pure-python resolver, which ignores cool-down-period"}, {"name":"PIPENV_PYPI_MIRROR","values":"any","effect":"replaces PyPI; on a mirror without upload times cool-down-period has no effect"}, {"name":"PIP_ONLY_BINARY","values":"any","effect":"sets only-binary of the pip Pipenv installs with, which no pip.conf reaches since 2026.3.0"}, {"name":"PIP_NO_BINARY","values":"any","effect":"builds these packages from source when installing, and pipenv install records no_binary for a named package in the Pipfile"} · source, verified 2026-10-31.

Pipenv · extra.resolverBypass ❓ uncertain

Not documented. Tested on 2026-10-30 with 2026.10.4: with resolver_backend = "pure-python" in [pipenv], pipenv lock locked the newest versions despite cool-down-period = "100000d"; resolver is the older name of the key.

  • {"table":"pipenv","keys":["resolver_backend","resolver"],"value":"pure-python"} · source, verified 2026-10-30.

Renovate · installScripts.allowList.setting ❓ uncertain

No per-package allow-list was found: scripts are all or nothing, via allowScripts: true (self-hosted global config) plus ignoreScripts: false.

Renovate · extra.pnpmSecurityExclusions ❓ uncertain

updatePnpmWorkspace: when a security update (vulnerability alert) changes a project with a pnpm lockfile whose pnpm-workspace.yaml sets minimumReleaseAge, Renovate adds the entry <name>@<version> to minimumReleaseAgeExclude with the comment "# Renovate security update: <name>@<version>" on the line above it; a later fix of the same package appends " || <version>" to the entry (and to the comment), and an entry for the package that has no comment gets one. The tests next to it (artifacts.spec.ts) show the written file. No other package manager (npm, Yarn, Bun, Deno) has such code at this tag; read from the code.

  • {"managers":["pnpm"],"file":"pnpm-workspace.yaml","setting":"minimumReleaseAgeExclude","comment":"# Renovate security update: ","versionSeparator":" || "} · source, verified 2026-10-30.

Renovate · extra.digestBypassFixed ⚠️ disputed

Digest updates could bypass minimumReleaseAge; the advisory and a maintainer comment give different fixed versions. Rebase requests could bypass strict checks before 44.61.3 (GHSA-2frq-pm48-6j4h).

  • Variant 1: 44.3.1 · source, verified 2026-10-28.
  • Variant 2: 44.10.0 · source, verified 2026-10-28.

Dependabot · installScripts.defaultPolicy ❓ uncertain

From dependabot-core source, not the docs: npm, Yarn and Bun lockfile updates run with scripts disabled; for pnpm only --lockfile-only was found. bundler, mix and pip may execute external code.

  • all versions: deny · source, verified 2026-10-28.

Dependabot · installScripts.disableAll ❓ uncertain

Nothing to configure for JavaScript ecosystems: scripts are always disabled (source code). For bundler, mix and pip, external code execution is disabled automatically when registries are configured.

  • null · source, verified 2026-10-28.

Dependabot · extra.yamlMergeKeys ❓ uncertain

Not known. The options reference does not mention YAML anchors or merge keys, GitHub parses dependabot.yml in its own service, and the dependabot-core pull request that would allow aliases ("we can't use the YAML anchor (aliases) feature in dependabot.yml file") is still open and marked stale (read 2026-10-31). depcurfew reads dependabot.yml without merge keys: a cooldown that an entry would get only through <<: *defaults counts as missing, which bot-cooldown reports, and depcurfew apply writes the cooldown into the entry itself, which wins over merged keys where they apply.

  • false · source, verified 2026-10-31.

Dependabot · extra.dayRange ⚠️ disputed

Matters for opting out of the default cooldown; cooldown.exclude: ["*"] works under the documented precedence rule either way. The announcement does not state its author's role.

  • Variant 1: 1 to 90 days · source, verified 2026-10-28.
  • Variant 2: 0 removes the cooldown · source, verified 2026-10-28.

Dependabot · extra.transitiveGating ❓ uncertain

Read from dependabot-core source.

  • Regular updates pass the cooldown to npm (11.10+), pnpm (10.16+) and Yarn Berry to hold back transitive dependencies; the Bun updater passes none · source, verified 2026-10-28.

Environment · corepack.defaults ⚠️ disputed

Corepack 0.36.0 runs the version lastKnownGood.json names for a package manager when no package.json above the current folder sets packageManager; without an entry it fetches the latest version of the npm package (yarn: Yarn Classic 1.x, which never reads .yarnrc.yml) and records it there, or with COREPACK_DEFAULT_TO_LATEST=0 uses its built-in default (getDefaultVersion in sources/Engine.ts). The code looks for the home folder in COREPACK_HOME, XDG_CACHE_HOME, LOCALAPPDATA (on every system) and then the home folder, using a variable that is set even when it is empty (??); the README leaves out XDG_CACHE_HOME and LOCALAPPDATA outside Windows. The code is what runs, so depcurfew follows it; the layout is the same since 0.13.0 (sources/folderUtils.ts), before which the folder was ~/.node/corepack. Values carry a hash after + (1.22.22+sha1.ac34...), which is not part of the version.

  • Variant 1: {"since":"0.13.0","locations":[{"platforms":["linux","darwin"],"directories":[{"from":{"env":"COREPACK_HOME","emptyCounts":true},"path":""},{"from":{"env":"XDG_CACHE_HOME","emptyCounts":true},"path":"node/corepack"},{"from":{"env":"LOCALAPPDATA","emptyCounts":true},"path":"node/corepack"},{"from":"home","path":".cache/node/corepack"}],"file":"lastKnownGood.json"},{"platforms":["win32"],"directories":[{"from":{"env":"COREPACK_HOME","emptyCounts":true},"path":""},{"from":{"env":"XDG_CACHE_HOME","emptyCounts":true},"path":"node/corepack"},{"from":{"env":"LOCALAPPDATA","emptyCounts":true},"path":"node/corepack"},{"from":"home","path":"AppData/Local/node/corepack"}],"file":"lastKnownGood.json"}]} · source, verified 2026-11-01. getCorepackHomeFolder: process.env.COREPACK_HOME ?? join(process.env.XDG_CACHE_HOME ?? process.env.LOCALAPPDATA ?? join(homedir(), process.platform === win32 ? AppData/Local : .cache), node/corepack); sources/Engine.ts reads join(getCorepackHomeFolder(), lastKnownGood.json), a JSON object of package manager names and versions.
  • Variant 2: {"since":"0.13.0","locations":[{"platforms":["linux","darwin"],"directories":[{"from":{"env":"COREPACK_HOME"},"path":""},{"from":"home","path":".cache/node/corepack"}],"file":"lastKnownGood.json"},{"platforms":["win32"],"directories":[{"from":{"env":"COREPACK_HOME"},"path":""},{"from":{"env":"LOCALAPPDATA"},"path":"node/corepack"}],"file":"lastKnownGood.json"}]} · source, verified 2026-11-01. "COREPACK_HOME can be set in order to define where Corepack should install the package managers. By default it is set to %LOCALAPPDATA%\node\corepack on Windows, and to $HOME/.cache/node/corepack everywhere else."

Environment · docker.dockerfilePrefix ❓ uncertain

  • Dockerfile. · source, verified 2026-11-01.

Environment · docker.nodeImageTags ❓ uncertain

Moving tags on 2026-10-28, when library/node was last generated: lts, lts-<codename>, current, latest and a variant alone (alpine, slim, bookworm) all mean 26.13.0. They move to another line without notice: after 26 became LTS no line is current, and how current and latest resolve once 27 is released is not documented. Tags of lines no longer built (20, iron, 18) are gone from library/node and are not updated any more.

  • {"tags":{"latest":"26","current":"26","lts":"26"}} · source, verified 2026-11-01.

Environment · docker.pythonImageTags ❓ uncertain

On 2026-11-01 latest, 3 and a variant alone (slim, alpine, trixie) mean 3.15.2 (aliases [3.15]='3 latest' in generate-stackbrew-library.sh); they move when a new line is released. X.Y-rc tags are prereleases.

  • {"tags":{"3":"3.15","latest":"3.15"}} · source, verified 2026-11-01.

Environment · npmRegistry.search ❓ uncertain

The description documents text, size (at most 250) and from. Results carried downloads.weekly on 2026-10-29, which the description does not list, and from was ignored, so one query returns at most 250 packages.

Environment · claudeCode.hookContext ❓ uncertain

The PreToolUse decision control table lists additionalContext in hookSpecificOutput as "String added to Claude's context alongside the tool result. Ignored when permissionDecision is "defer"", next to permissionDecision, permissionDecisionReason and updatedInput, and #add-context-for-claude says Claude Code wraps the string in a system reminder that Claude reads on the next model request, places it next to the tool result for PreToolUse, writes a value over 10,000 characters to a file and passes its path with a preview of 2,000 characters, and recommends factual statements over imperative instructions. Uncertain: the page does not say in so many words that additionalContext works without permissionDecision; the table ties it to no decision but defer, and an output without permissionDecision leaves the call to the normal permission flow.

  • {"field":"additionalContext","withoutDecision":true,"inlineLimit":10000} · source, verified 2026-11-01.
